For the size of the loss, which a fade forecast needs, the slope is set-specific (0.34 to 0.37 on the two LG M50 parameterisations, 0.58 on `Ai2020`, 0.7 to 0.9 on `NCA_Kim2011`). That is a reason to derive S from the plating law rather than to fit it, and a test for any derivation: it must give about 0.36 on `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` and about 0.58 on `Ai2020`. On these three pools the cycle-10 energy alone already carries the order, so a screen test needs pools where the best design is not near the top at cycle 10 (fade-limited `Ai2020` currents, or a slower set).
- Caveats: three pools, one per set (sign p = 0.25); the screen part is untested here (best design in raw top 3); `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` are two parameterisations of one cell (LG M50; identical positive-electrode OCP in the tables here, different negative-electrode OCP), not two independent cells; plating parameters are OKane2022's on every set (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/crossset/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `crossset.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5w: does 5v's self-limiting correction carry to other chemistries when only its intercept is recalibrated, on 8 designs per pool, with the slope kept from Ai2020? (plan, written before any fresh run; H126 to H130 are this entry's)
- Question: 5v's corrected map beat 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on three fresh `Ai2020` pools with A = 0.658, S = 0.577 frozen, but on the set the constants came from. Its next step asks whether the correction carries to `NCA_Kim2011`, `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` with constants self-calibrated on 8 designs. A practitioner with a new cell needs to know how many 100-cycle runs that costs and which constant must move.
- Dev, seen (`python experiments/crossset/run.py dev`, stdlib, 50 random draws of 8 calibration designs on each committed 5u and 5v pool, ranks on the 45 to 56 held-out designs): refitting both A and S on 8 designs is unstable, with S from &minus;0.42 to 2.08 on 5u's `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` pool (held-out map rank median 0.616, worst &minus;0.081) and 0.39 to 0.82 on the six `Ai2020` pools (worst 0.78 to 0.90). Refitting only A with S held at 0.577 gives held-out medians 0.922 to 0.974 on `Ai2020` (worst 0.875) and 0.537 on `NCA_Kim2011` (worst 0.475), against the &radic;cycle fit's 0.59 to 0.75 and &minus;0.15. On `NCA_Kim2011` raw cycle-10 Wh/kg ranks at 0.914, above any fade list. On the 5s diagnostic designs (8 `Chen2020@0.5x`, 6 `OKane2022@1x`; too few to split) the frozen `Ai2020` constants leave a median |ln error| of 0.054 and 0.128 on the cycle-99 lithium loss, and the in-sample slopes are 0.47 and 0.38.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/crossset/run.py`): per pool, 5u's law choice and the cycle-99 law loss as in 5v; 8 completed designs drawn with `random.Random(0)` are run to cycle 100 and set A = mean of ln(L_law / L_DFN) &minus; 0.577 ln(L_law / n_Li); then L_corr and 5s's map as in 5v. Everything is scored on the other designs only (held out).
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 16, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `NCA_Kim2011@0.875x`, `Chen2020@1.5x` and `OKane2022@1.5x`, 192 designs; DFN with SEI + plating to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 2 workers. Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted. Balance tables were built (no simulation) before this entry.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt energy fit and union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4), 5v's frozen map, the two-constant refit on the same 8 designs, raw cycle-10 Wh/kg.
- H126 (ranks): Spearman(one-constant map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) on held-out designs above the sqrt fit's on all 3 pools. Refuted if on 1 or fewer.
- H127 (lithium size): median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| at most 0.06 on every pool. Refuted if above 0.10 on any.
- H128 (the intercept must move): with 5v's frozen `Ai2020` constants the same error exceeds 0.06 on at least 2 of 3 pools. Refuted if at most 0.06 on all 3.
- H129 (screen): the union of raw top 4 and one-constant top 4 within 0.5% of the held-out pool's DFN best on every pool. Refuted if above 2% on any.
- H130 (one constant is enough): the one-constant map ranks at least as well as the two-constant refit on all 3. Refuted if on 1 or fewer.
- Reported beside, not counted: the same metrics over 50 calibration draws (median and worst), the pool's in-sample slope, the map fed DFN's own loss.
- Caveats fixed now: three pools, one per set (3 of 3 gives sign-test p = 0.25); the slope is still fitted, not derived; plating parameters are OKane2022's on every set (5p); open-circuit map, which 5t found under-predicts fade on the NMC811 sets; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); the calibration designs cost 8 extra 100-cycle runs per new pool, on top of the union's resumed designs.
- Script: `experiments/crossset/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5v outcome: the map's failure was conditioning, and one self-limiting correction fixes it out of sample. With A = 0.658, S = 0.577 frozen from 5u's pools, the corrected map ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy at 0.92, 0.96 and 0.96 on three fresh `Ai2020` pools (5n's &radic;cycle energy fit 0.59 to 0.67, 5u's uncorrected map 0.61 to 0.82) and gets the cycle-99 lithium loss to a median 4% (uncorrected 11 to 18%). H122, H123, H124 supported, H125 mixed
- Bench: 192 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 15, 2 workers, about 54 minutes on 4 cores; no memory kills. 3 designs dropped by the solver (IDA failures, 2 on `Ai2020@0.35x`, 1 on `@0.45x`), so 62, 63 and 64 were scored. The report was run once, after the bench finished.
- H122 supported: Spearman(corrected map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) 0.918, 0.955, 0.963 on `Ai2020` 0.35&times;, 0.45&times;, 0.6&times;, above the sqrt fit's 0.670, 0.618, 0.590 on all three (sign test p = 0.25 at n = 3) and within 0.01 to 0.04 of the map fed DFN's own loss (0.961, 0.963, 0.969). The uncorrected 5u map ranks at 0.610, 0.681, 0.816.
- H123 supported: median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| 0.039, 0.036, 0.040 (bar 0.06); uncorrected 0.112, 0.158, 0.182.
- H124 supported: the union of raw top 4 and corrected-map top 4 found the pool's DFN best on all three (gap 0), as did 5n's union, at 6.3 resumed designs on average against 7.7.
- H125 mixed: a 10% lognormal error on the true loss lowers the map's Spearman by 0.52, 0.37 and 0.19 (bar 0.2 on every pool; refuted below 0.1). The map is ill-conditioned at low current and less so at 0.6&times;, where the fade is smaller (median 0.43 against 0.50).
- Descriptive (in the plan, not counted): the uncorrected law ranks the cycle-99 lithium loss itself at 0.989, 0.976, 0.970 (corrected 0.993, 0.990, 0.989), so the order of the loss was never the problem; its error ratio rises with the true lost fraction (Spearman 0.89, 0.86, 0.79), as on dev. Refitted on 8 random designs per pool run to cycle 100 the constants are A = 0.61 to 0.72, S = 0.57 to 0.60 (frozen 0.658, 0.577) and the self-calibrated map ranks at 0.934, 0.947, 0.961: a user calibrating on a new pool loses nothing measurable here.
- Not in the plan, read off the same table: raw cycle-10 Wh/kg alone ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.534, 0.884 and 0.976, above the corrected map on `Ai2020@0.6x` (0.963). As fade shrinks with current, the cycle-10 energy carries the order and any fade list matters less; the correction matters where fade decides (0.35&times;: 0.918 against 0.534).
- Reading: 5u's diagnosis was half right. The per-design extrapolation was the problem, but not its ranking: the map amplifies a size error (a 10% random error costs 0.2 to 0.5 of Spearman), and the ten-cycle law's size error is systematic, larger for designs that lose more, as a self-limiting loss would be. A two-constant line in log space removes it, and constants fitted on one set of three pools carry to three fresh pools of the same set, two at currents no milestone ran. The map with this correction is the first ten-cycle fade predictor in the programme to beat 5n's energy fit out of sample on every pool; 5n's union now has a cheaper equal (6.3 designs resumed, not 7.7).
- Caveats: three pools of one set, the set the constants came from; `NCA_Kim2011` needs its own slope (0.81 on dev) and `Chen2020` / `OKane2022` are untested; the correction is empirical, with no mechanism fitted (the slope 0.58 is not derived); plating parameters are OKane2022's (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); modelled fade only.
- Output: `experiments/calmap/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `calmap.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5v: is the balance map's ranking failure a conditioning problem, and does one self-limiting correction of the ten-cycle lithium law, fitted on 5u's Ai2020 pools and frozen, carry to fresh Ai2020 pools? (plan, written before any fresh run; H122 to H125 are this entry's)
- Question: 5u's reading was that the per-design ten-cycle law gets the cycle-99 lithium loss wrong and the next predictor "should be judged on the lithium Spearman first". Dev on 5u's committed pools (seen; `python experiments/calmap/run.py dev`, stdlib) says that is the wrong target: the &radic;n / power law already ranks the cycle-99 DFN lithium loss at Spearman 0.98 to 0.99 on all four pools (the cycle-10 loss alone ranks it at 0.98 to 0.998). The map is ill-conditioned: fed the true loss times exp(N(0, 0.1)) it ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.38, 0.54, 0.67 and 0.48 (mean of 20 draws) against 0.95 to 0.97 with the exact loss, and a uniform 1.25&times; over-prediction alone drops it to 0.65 to 0.74. So what decides the ranking is the per-design size of the loss to within a few percent, not its order.
- Dev, seen: the law's error is systematic, not noise. L_law(99) / L_DFN(99) rises with the predicted fraction of lithium lost (Spearman with the true fraction 0.93, 0.88, 0.82 on `Ai2020` 0.3&times;, 0.4&times;, 0.5&times;; &minus;0.49 on `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x`): designs that lose more slow down more. One line, ln(L_law / L_DFN) = A + S ln(L_law / n_Li), fitted pooled on the three `Ai2020` pools gives A = 0.658, S = 0.577 (per-pool slopes 0.57, 0.60, 0.59). In sample it cuts the median |ln error| from 0.12 to 0.18 down to 0.026 to 0.038 and lifts the map's energy ranking from 0.64 to 0.76 to 0.90, 0.97 and 0.97 (true-loss map 0.97, 0.97, 0.96). On `NCA_Kim2011` the slope is 0.81 and the pooled `Ai2020` constants give 0.58; the correction is a per-set calibration, and this milestone tests it on `Ai2020` only. Also seen: refitting the law to cycles 2 to 20 (a cycle-20 rung, 5u's other suggestion) narrows the ratio spread a little but ranks the energy worse (0.60 to 0.71 on `Ai2020`), so it is not taken further. 5u's first suggestion, one curvature per pool (the law fitted to the pool's median normalised curve) with a per-design scale (the cycle-10 loss, or a least-squares scale over cycles 2 to 10), ranks the energy at 0.62 to 0.75 on `Ai2020` and 0.37 to 0.40 on `NCA_Kim2011`, no better than the per-design law, because it keeps the same size error. Nothing beyond these numbers was computed before this entry (the dev scripts are in `experiments/calmap/run.py dev` for the correction; the curvature and cycle-20 checks were scratch and are not committed).
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/calmap/run.py`): 5u's per-pool law (median ten-cycle exponent &ge; 0.6 takes a + b&radic;n, else a n<sup>b</sup>), read at cycle 99, then L_corr = L_law / exp(0.658 + 0.577 ln(L_law / n_Li)), then 5s's map unchanged (cycle-10 Wh/kg &times; E_ocv(n_Li &minus; L_corr) / E_ocv(n_Li &minus; lost by end of cycle 9), floored at the cycle-9 loss). Only cycles 1 to 10 of each design are read; A and S are frozen.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 15, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `Ai2020@0.35x` (a current 5s ran, new designs), `Ai2020@0.45x` and `Ai2020@0.6x` (currents no milestone ran), 192 designs. DFN with SEI + plating to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 2 workers (5u: three ran out of 15 GB). Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt energy fit (cycles 5 to 10, read at 100), 5n's union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4), 5u's uncorrected map.
- H122 (ranks): Spearman(corrected map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) above the sqrt fit's on all 3 pools and at least 0.85 on each. Refuted if below the sqrt fit on 2 or more, or below 0.75 on any.
- H123 (lithium size): median |ln(L_corr(99) / L_DFN(99))| at most 0.06 on every pool. Refuted if above 0.10 on any.
- H124 (screen): the union of raw top 4 and corrected-map top 4 within 0.5% of the pool's DFN best and no more than 0.5 points worse than 5n's union on every pool. Refuted if above 2% on any.
- H125 (conditioning, replicated): a 10% lognormal error on the true cycle-99 loss lowers the map's Spearman by at least 0.2 on every pool (mean of 20 draws). Refuted if by less than 0.1 on any.
- Reported beside, not counted: a self-calibrated variant (A, S refitted on 8 random designs of the same pool run to cycle 100, the cost a user would pay on a new cell set), the uncorrected map, the true-loss map, the lithium Spearman of each.
- Caveats fixed now: three pools (3 of 3 gives sign-test p = 0.25); one set (`Ai2020`), the set the constants came from; the correction is empirical, a line in log space with no mechanism fitted; plating parameters are OKane2022's (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Script: `experiments/calmap/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5u outcome: on four fresh pools (256 unseen designs) the per-pool law choice works and the &radic;n law gets the median cycle-99 lithium loss right out of sample (0.94 to 1.05 on `Ai2020`), but the map still ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy worse than 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on 3 of 4 pools. Fed DFN's own loss the same map ranks at 0.95 to 0.97 on all four, so what is left is the per-design lithium extrapolation (10th to 90th percentile 0.77 to 1.45 of the truth). H121 supported, H118 mixed, H117, H119 and H120 refuted. 5n's union stays the procedure
- Bench: 256 DFN runs (SEI + plating) to cycle 100, Sobol seed 14, about 90 minutes on 4 cores. Three workers ran out of memory (two runs killed by the kernel, exit &minus;9; amended before any result was read: 2 workers, killed runs retried, the predictor untouched). 15 designs dropped by the solver (IDA failures, 11 on `Ai2020@0.3x`, 4 on `Ai2020@0.4x`, the same first-cycle failures 5l and 5n saw below 0.4&times;), so 53, 60, 64 and 64 designs were scored. The report code was run once on the finished `Ai2020@0.3x` pool with its output discarded, to check it ran, before the other pools finished.
- H121 supported: the selector picked &radic;n on the three `Ai2020` pools (b_med 0.86, 0.88, 0.90) and the power law on `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` (b_med 0.25), as 5s's pools predicted. Median L_law(99) / L_DFN(99): 1.05, 0.99 and 0.94 on `Ai2020` 0.3&times;, 0.4&times;, 0.5&times; (bar 0.8 to 1.25), 1.32 on `NCA_Kim2011` with the power law (5t's &radic;n law was 1.66 there on dev). The law is right on the median on designs nobody had looked at.
- H117 refuted: Spearman(map, DFN@100) above the sqrt fit's on 1 of 4 (`Ai2020@0.5x`, 0.764 against 0.666). `Ai2020@0.3x` 0.641 against 0.667, `@0.4x` 0.662 against 0.745, `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` &minus;0.352 against &minus;0.157 (both below the raw cycle-10 energy's 0.914 there).
- H118 mixed, H119 refuted: the union of raw top 4 and map top 4 found the DFN best on three pools and missed by 1.25% on `Ai2020@0.4x`, where the best ranks 22nd by the map and 1st by the sqrt fit; 5n's union is exact there and misses by 0.87% on `Ai2020@0.5x` (where the map's union is exact). Excess over 5n's union: +1.25 points at worst (bar 1).
- H120 refuted: median |fade error| from cycle 10 to 100 below the sqrt fit's on 1 of 4 (`NCA_Kim2011`, 0.40 against 0.70); 0.080, 0.103 and 0.123 against 0.075, 0.079 and 0.107 on `Ai2020`.
- Descriptive (in the plan, not counted): fed DFN's own cycle-99 lithium loss the map ranks the 100-cycle energy at 0.974, 0.968, 0.961 and 0.952, with median fade error 0.016 to 0.033 on all four pools, including `NCA_Kim2011`, where every energy extrapolation ranks negatively. 5s's decomposition, checked on 27 designs, holds on 241 unseen ones. The law the selector did not pick ranks worse on every pool (0.28 to 0.43 on `Ai2020` with the power law; &minus;0.49 on `NCA_Kim2011` with &radic;n). The three-list union (raw + sqrt + map, 4 each) is exact on all four pools at 11.0 resumed designs against 7.8 for 5n's; the top 8 by raw cycle-10 energy alone misses by 2.74% and 1.44% on `Ai2020` 0.3&times; and 0.4&times;.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts): the per-design ratio L_law(99) / L_DFN(99) runs from 0.77 to 0.89 at the 10th percentile to 1.28 to 1.45 at the 90th on `Ai2020` (1.19 to 1.50 on `NCA_Kim2011`). Getting the pool median right is not enough: this per-design spread is what separates the map's 0.64 to 0.76 from its 0.95 to 0.97 when fed the true loss.
- Reading: the decomposition is right and now tested out of sample: energy fade at cycle 100 is the electrode-balance map of the lithium lost (Spearman 0.95 to 0.97 on four fresh pools). The pool-level law choice from the ten-cycle exponent works. What fails is ranking designs by a two-parameter law fitted per design to cycles 2 to 10: the curvature that decides the late loss is not identifiable from ten cycles for each design. The next predictor should share information across a pool (one curvature per pool, fitted to the median curve, with a per-design scale) or read one later rung (cycle 20) where the deceleration has begun, and should be judged on the lithium Spearman first, since the map turns a good lithium ranking into a good energy ranking.
- Caveats: four pools (4 of 4 would give sign-test p = 0.125); one Sobol pool per condition; 15 solver drops on the two lowest-current pools; plating parameters are OKane2022's on both sets (5p); open-circuit map; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` not tested.
- Output: `experiments/freshlaw/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `freshlaw.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5u: on fresh pools nobody has seen, does the balance map with a lithium-loss law chosen per pool from the ten-cycle curve rank the 100-cycle DFN energy and carry the union screen? (plan, written before any run; H117 to H121 are this entry's)
- Question: 5t's &radic;n law ranked better than 5s's power law on 12 of 13 committed pools, but it was chosen on 27 designs of the same family, scored against truth read since 5b, and over-predicted `NCA_Kim2011`'s decelerating loss. Both open items in 5t's next step meet here: choose the law from the curve itself, and test on pools where neither truth nor lithium curve has been seen.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/freshlaw/run.py`): per pool, b_med = median over designs of 5s's power-law exponent of the lithium lost by the end of cycles 2 to 10. b_med &ge; 0.6 takes L(n) = a + b&radic;n (5t), b_med &lt; 0.6 the power law a n<sup>b</sup> (5s). On 5s's 17 pools b_med was 0.84 to 0.96 on every graphite pool and 0.23 to 0.30 on `NCA_Kim2011`; 0.6 sits in that gap and nothing was tuned on fade or energy. Then 5s's map unchanged: predicted cycle-100 Wh/kg = cycle-10 Wh/kg &times; E_ocv(n_Li &minus; L(99)) / E_ocv(n_Li &minus; lost by end of cycle 9), L(99) floored at the cycle-9 loss.
- Fresh pools, fixed now: 64 scrambled Sobol designs (seed 14, used by no milestone; energy-cell bounds) on `Ai2020@0.3x`, `Ai2020@0.4x` (a current no milestone ran), `Ai2020@0.5x` and `NCA_Kim2011@0.625x` (a current no milestone ran), 256 designs. DFN with SEI + plating (5r's `both`) to cycle 100 with the lithium curve, one fresh process per run, 3 workers (four ran out of memory in 5s's diagnostic). The screen reads cycles 1 to 10 of the same run; 5s showed a separate ten-cycle run reproduces them exactly. Designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped and counted.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt energy fit (cycles 5 to 10, read at 100) and 5n's union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4).
- H117 (ranks): Spearman(map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) above the sqrt fit's on at least 3 of 4 pools. Refuted if on 2 or fewer.
- H118 (screen): the union of raw top 4 and map top 4 within 1% of the pool's DFN best on every pool. Refuted if above 3% on any.
- H119 (against 5n): that union's gap no larger than 5n's union's on every pool. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- H120 (fade size): median |predicted &minus; DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100| below the sqrt fit's on at least 3 of 4. Refuted if on 2 or fewer.
- H121 (the law out of sample): the selector picks the power law on `NCA_Kim2011` and &radic;n on the three `Ai2020` pools, and on each `Ai2020` pool the median L_law(99) / L_DFN(99) is within 0.8 to 1.25 (5t's dev: 1.04 on `Ai2020@0.3x`). Refuted if the selector picks otherwise or a ratio is outside 0.67 to 1.5.
- Reported beside, not counted: the map with the law not picked, the map fed DFN's own cycle-99 loss (5s's decomposition, now on 256 unseen designs), the three-list union, and the top 8 by raw cycle-10 energy (a cost-matched list with no fade model).
- Caveats fixed now: four pools, so a sign test cannot reach p &lt; 0.05 (4 of 4 gives p = 0.125); one Sobol pool per condition; plating parameters are OKane2022's on `Ai2020` and `NCA_Kim2011` (5p); the map is open-circuit; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); `Chen2020` and `OKane2022`, where 5r and 5t found a rate effect, are not tested.
- Script: `experiments/freshlaw/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5t outcome: the &radic;n loss law fixes the map's ranking (better than 5s's power law on 12 of 13 test pools, sign test p = 0.003) and ranks the 100-cycle DFN energy better than 5n's &radic;cycle energy fit on 8 of 13, one short of the bar; its fade size is worse on 7 of 13 and, as the union's fade list, it misses by up to 3.2%. H116 supported, H112 mixed, H113 to H115 refuted. 5n's union stays the procedure
- Bench: none. The report reads 5s's committed ten-cycle DFN runs (bench.jsonl, 1,108), balance tables and the committed 100-cycle pools, and runs in about 2 s with the standard library. 5s's own report regenerates byte for byte from the same files.
- H112 mixed: Spearman(&radic;n map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) above the sqrt fit's on 8 of 13 test conditions (bar 9; refuted at 6 or fewer; sign test p = 0.58). Wins: `Ai2020` 0.275&times;, 0.35&times;, 0.4375&times;, 0.5&times;, 0.625&times; (0.809 against 0.578), `Chen2020` 1&times; and 2&times; (0.901 against 0.572), `OKane2022@2x` (0.897 against 0.570). Losses: `Ai2020` 0.25&times; (0.605 against 0.647) and 0.375&times; (0.671 against 0.676), `OKane2022@1.25x` (0.866 against 0.896) and both `NCA_Kim2011` pools (&minus;0.45 and &minus;0.46 against &minus;0.02 and &minus;0.33).
- H113 refuted: median |fade error| below the sqrt fit's on 6 of 13 (four `Ai2020`, both `NCA_Kim2011`, the latter only because both are far off: 0.62 to 0.63 against 0.69). On `Chen2020` and `OKane2022` the map under-predicts the fade (median 0.08 to 0.21 against DFN's 0.24 to 0.37), the same direction 5r saw over 20 cycles; on `NCA_Kim2011` it saturates (0.97 to 0.98 against 0.34 to 0.36), because the law over-predicts that set's decelerating loss (it did by 1.66 on dev).
- H114 refuted: the union of raw top 4 and &radic;n-map top 4 missed the DFN best by 3.17% on `Ai2020@0.275x` (the bar is 3%), 2.17% on `@0.5x` and 1.19% on `@0.25x`; exact or within 0.08% on the other 10.
- H115 refuted: worse than 5n's union by up to 3.0 points (`Ai2020@0.275x`, where 5n's misses by 0.16%).
- H116 supported: the &radic;n map ranks better than 5s's power-law map on 12 of 13 (p = 0.003); the exception is `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x` (&minus;0.45 against &minus;0.26). Read at cycle 99 the power law predicts 1.8 to 2.2 times the &radic;n law's loss on the graphite pools and 0.78 to 0.80 of it on `NCA_Kim2011`.
- Descriptive, not in the plan (the three-list union, top 4 each by raw, sqrt fit and &radic;n map, written into the script before its first run): within 0.16% on all 13 test pools, the same worst case as 5n's union, at a mean 9.8 resumed designs against 7.4. It buys nothing here.
- Dev (seen): with the &radic;n law the map's mean fade error on the 27 diagnostic designs is 0.14 (`Ai2020@0.3x`), 0.07 (`Chen2020@0.5x`), 0.18 (`OKane2022@1x`) and 0.61 (`NCA_Kim2011@1x`), against 0.02 to 0.12 fed DFN's own loss (5s): getting L(99) right on the median is not enough, because the per-design ratio still spreads (0.93 to 1.75 on `Ai2020@0.3x`) and the map is steep where the window shrinks.
- Reading: a decelerating loss law is most of what the map was missing as a ranker. On the eight graphite pools where the sqrt fit ranks below 0.70 (`Ai2020` 0.25&times; to 0.4375&times;, 0.625&times;, `Chen2020@2x`, `OKane2022@2x`) the &radic;n map is at 0.61 to 0.90, and by &ge; 0.3 above on the two 2&times; pools. It is not yet a fade-size predictor (rate effect on NMC811 sets, saturation on NCA) and not a safe fade list on its own (3.2% miss). The useful next step is a per-set law choice made from the curve itself (the power-law exponent over cycles 2 to 10, a pool median of 0.23 to 0.30 on `NCA_Kim2011` against 0.84 to 0.96 on the graphite pools in 5s, already separates them), and a test on fresh pools where neither the truth nor the law's dev has been seen.
- Caveats: committed pools (truth seen since 5b); the law was chosen on 27 designs of four conditions from the same 17-pool family; no new simulation; 9 of the 17 pools came in through 5s's Wh/Wh-per-kg selection slip, so 7 of the 13 test pools (`Ai2020@0.625x`, both `Chen2020`, both `NCA_Kim2011`, both `OKane2022`) have the DFN best inside the raw top 4 by Wh/kg and test the fade list less; all three union misses are on the other six; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Output: `experiments/losslaw/results.txt`, `summary.json`, `losslaw.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5t: does a &radic;n lithium-loss law, fitted to the same ten DFN cycles, fix 5s's over-prediction well enough for the balance map to be the union screen's fade list? (plan, written before any test condition's prediction under the new law was computed; H112 to H116 are this entry's)
- Question: 5s found the balance map right (within a mean 0.02 to 0.05 of DFN's 100-cycle fade on three of four diagnostic conditions when fed DFN's own lithium loss) and the power law wrong (cycle-99 loss over-predicted 1.2 to 2.8 times, because plating slows). If a decelerating law fitted to the same cycles 2 to 10 gets cycle-99 loss right, the map becomes a fade list at no new simulation cost: everything needed (1,108 ten-cycle lithium curves, balance tables, committed 100-cycle truth) is already committed.
- Development, seen before this entry (the 27 designs 5s's post hoc diagnostic ran to cycle 100 with their lithium curves, on `Ai2020@0.3x`, `NCA_Kim2011@1x`, `Chen2020@0.5x`, `OKane2022@1x`): median predicted / DFN lithium lost by cycle 99, per condition in that order, for six laws fitted to the end-of-cycle loss: power law a n<sup>b</sup> on cycles 2 to 10 (5s) 1.93, 1.36, 1.91, 1.61; the same on cycles 6 to 10 1.74, 1.22, 1.68, 1.52; a quadratic in log-log on cycles 2 to 10 (exponent floored at 0) 1.40, 0.91, 1.29, 1.38; a power law of the per-cycle loss on cycles 3 to 10, summed 1.57, 1.02, 1.50, 1.44; a + b&radic;n on cycles 6 to 10 1.13, 1.47, 1.08, 0.84; **a + b&radic;n on cycles 2 to 10 1.04, 1.66, 1.00, 0.75**. Chosen on that: the last, the SEI-growth form, nearest 1 on the three graphite conditions. Nothing else from the diagnostic (no fade, rank or screen number under any new law) was computed before this entry.
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/losslaw/run.py`): L(n) = a + b&radic;n, least squares through the run's "Total lithium lost" at the end of cycles 2 to 10, read at n = 99 and floored at the cycle-9 loss; then 5s's map unchanged: predicted cycle-100 Wh/kg = cycle-10 Wh/kg &times; E_ocv(n_Li &minus; L(99)) / E_ocv(n_Li &minus; lost by end of cycle 9). No constant fitted to fade or energy; the law's choice is the only thing tuned, on lithium only.
- Test conditions, fixed now: 5s's 17 minus the four diagnostic ones, 13 conditions, 902 designs (`Ai2020` 0.25&times;, 0.275&times;, 0.35&times;, 0.375&times;, 0.4375&times;, 0.5&times;, 0.625&times;; `Chen2020` 1&times;, 2&times;; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.5&times;, 0.75&times;; `OKane2022` 1.25&times;, 2&times;). Their 100-cycle truth was read by 5b to 5s; their lithium beyond cycle 10 has never been simulated. The four diagnostic conditions are reported beside, not counted.
- Comparators: 5n's sqrt fit of the energy (cycles 5 to 10, read at 100), 5n's union (top 4 raw plus top 4 sqrt), and 5s's power-law map.
- H112 (ranks): Spearman(&radic;n map, DFN@100 Wh/kg) above the sqrt fit's on at least 9 of 13. Refuted if on 6 or fewer.
- H113 (fade size): median |predicted &minus; DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100| below the sqrt fit's on at least 9 of 13. Refuted if on 6 or fewer.
- H114 (screen): the union of the top 4 raw and the top 4 by the &radic;n map is within 1% of the pool's DFN best on every test condition. Refuted if above 3% on any.
- H115 (against 5n): that union's gap is no larger than 5n's union's on every test condition. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- H116 (the law is the fix): the &radic;n map's Spearman with DFN@100 is above 5s's power-law map's on at least 10 of 13. Refuted if on 6 or fewer.
- Caveats fixed now: committed pools (truth seen by earlier milestones, predictor unseen); the law was picked on 27 designs from four conditions, and it over-predicts `NCA_Kim2011@1x`'s loss there by 1.66, so `NCA_Kim2011` test pools are expected to be its weakest; the map is open-circuit (5r's H107); plating parameters are OKane2022's on six of seven sets; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); no new simulation, so nothing here checks the fresh-run reproducibility that 5s already did.
- Script: `experiments/losslaw/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5s outcome: the balance map is right, the lithium extrapolation is not. A power law through ten DFN cycles' lithium loss over-predicts the cycle-99 loss 1.2 to 2.8 times, so the map's predicted fade ranks 100-cycle DFN worse than 5n's sqrt fit on 15 of 17 conditions and the union with it misses by up to 6.9%. H108 to H111 refuted
- Bench: 1,108 ten-cycle DFN runs (SEI + plating), 4 workers, about 28 minutes on 4 cores; none failed. Cycle-10 Wh/kg reproduces every committed run exactly (max relative difference 0). The map costs under a millisecond per design once each set's OCP table exists (four sets, 1.6 s in all), so as a fade list it would be free.
- H108 refuted: Spearman(map prediction, DFN@100) beat the sqrt fit's on 2 of 17 (`NCA_Kim2011@1x` &minus;0.27 against &minus;0.60, `OKane2022@2x` 0.63 against 0.57). On `Ai2020` 0.25&times; to 0.5&times; the map ranks at 0.27 to 0.55 against 0.57 to 0.76 for the sqrt fit; on `Chen2020@0.5x` it is anti-correlated (&minus;0.46).
- H109 refuted: the map's median |fade error| from cycle 10 to 100 was below the sqrt fit's on 7 of 17, the three `NCA_Kim2011` conditions, where the sqrt fit itself fails (0.38 to 0.41 against 0.50 to 0.69), `Chen2020@2x` and the three `OKane2022` pools; on none of the eight `Ai2020` pools. On `Ai2020` the map predicts a median fade of 0.87 to 0.99 against DFN's 0.45 to 0.56.
- H110 refuted: the union of the raw top 4 and the map's top 4 missed the pool's DFN best by up to 6.88%, above 3% on three conditions (`Ai2020` 0.25&times; 6.88%, 0.275&times; 3.17%, 0.3&times; 5.58%).
- H111 refuted: it was worse than 5n's union on 7 conditions, every `Ai2020` pool from 0.25&times; to 0.5&times;, by up to 6.88 points. 5n's union (raw top 4 plus sqrt top 4) is exact on 13 of the 17 and within 0.32% on all.
- Amended after the first report, before this entry: 5m's and 5n's committed pools store Wh/kg with mass 1; the first report divided the fresh runs' Wh by that 1 and so ranked those five pools in Wh. It now divides by each design's stack mass (recorded by `tables`, pybamm parameter values only), and the reproduction check is in Wh/kg. That moved `Ai2020@0.275x`, `@0.3x`, `@0.35x` and `@0.4375x`; no verdict changed (H108 was 3 of 17 before, 2 after). The same slip was in the condition rule: it ranked committed cycle-10 energy in Wh, so 9 of the 17 have the DFN best inside the raw top 4 by Wh/kg. On the 8 that meet the rule as meant (seven `Ai2020` pools and `NCA_Kim2011@1x`) the map beats the sqrt fit on ranking on 1 and the union's worst gap is 6.88% against 0.32% for 5n's.
- Post hoc, why (after the verdicts; `diag` in the script): DFN to cycle 100 with the lithium curve on the first 8 designs of `Ai2020@0.3x`, `NCA_Kim2011@1x`, `Chen2020@0.5x` and `OKane2022@1x`, 27 of 32 completed (five were killed for memory with four parallel 100-cycle runs, not by the solver; cycle-100 Wh/kg reproduces the committed runs exactly). Fed DFN's own cycle-99 lithium loss, the map's fade from cycle 10 to 100 is within a mean 0.021 of DFN on `Ai2020@0.3x`, 0.045 on `Chen2020@0.5x`, 0.051 on `NCA_Kim2011@1x` and 0.120 on `OKane2022@1x` (under-predicting there, as 5r saw over 20 cycles). Fed the power law it is off by 0.43, 0.34, 0.37 and 0.08. The power law over-predicts the cycle-99 loss by a median 1.93, 1.91, 1.36 and 1.61 times because plating slows down: the local exponent falls from 0.77 to 0.95 over cycles 2 to 10 to 0.24 to 0.63 over cycles 50 to 99 on the graphite cells (0.24 to 0.37 falling to 0.14 to 0.16 on `NCA_Kim2011`).
- Reading: 5r's decomposition holds at 100 cycles where the cell runs near open circuit (mean error 0.02 to 0.05 on three of four conditions), so the remaining problem is the lithium-loss rate, which decelerates in a way ten cycles do not show. A predictor needs a self-limiting loss law (plating that slows as the negative empties, which 5q's OCP-rise term tried on SPMe's plating) fitted to the early curve, or a later rung (cycle 20 or 30) where the deceleration has begun. Until then 5o's salt floor plus 5n's union screen stays the procedure.
- Caveats: committed pools (truth read by 5b to 5o; the predictor's output was not); the diagnostic is 27 designs on four conditions, chosen after the verdicts; plating parameters are OKane2022's on six of seven sets; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Output: `experiments/mapscreen/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `diag.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `mapscreen.svg`.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 5s: does 5r's balance map, fed a power law of ten DFN cycles' own lithium loss, predict 100-cycle DFN energy well enough to be the union screen's fade list? (plan, written before any ten-cycle run's lithium curve was read; H108 to H111 are this entry's)
- Question: 5r split fade into a design-only electrode-balance map and a lithium-loss rate, but fed the map DFN's own 20-cycle loss. 5n's union screen already runs every design to cycle 10, so its lithium curve is free. If a power law through that curve, mapped to energy with 5r's map, predicts cycle 100, it can replace 5n's sqrt(cycle) energy extrapolation as the fade list at almost no cost (the map, about 0.05 s per design).
- Predictor, fixed now (`experiments/mapscreen/run.py`): L(n) = a n<sup>b</sup>, log-log least squares through the run's "Total lithium lost [mol]" at the end of cycles 2 to 10 (parallel count included, 5p); predicted cycle-100 Wh/kg = cycle-10 Wh/kg &times; E_ocv(n_Li &minus; L(99)) / E_ocv(n_Li &minus; lost by end of cycle 9), with E_ocv 5r's open-circuit energy between the cut-offs (cycle n's discharge holds the lithium left after cycle n&minus;1). No fitted constant, no rate term. A run with fewer than three positive loss points is predicted not to fade.
- Conditions, fixed now by a rule on committed data: every committed 100-cycle pool whose median DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100 is at least 0.2 and whose DFN-best design ranks below 4th by raw cycle-10 Wh/kg (so the union's fade list has to carry it): 17 conditions, 1,108 designs (`Ai2020` 0.25&times; to 0.625&times;, eight; `Chen2020` 0.5&times;, 1&times;, 2&times;; `NCA_Kim2011` 0.5&times;, 0.75&times;, 1&times;; `OKane2022` 1&times;, 1.25&times;, 2&times;). The 100-cycle truth was read by milestones 5b to 5o; the lithium curves and the predictor's output on them were not.
- Runs: DFN with SEI + plating (5r's `both`, TR.OPTS) to cycle 10 on every design, one fresh process per run, 4 workers; check: cycle-10 Wh reproduces the committed run's (a smoke run on `Chen2020@1x` design 0 matched exactly).
- Comparator: 5n's sqrt fit (a line in &radic;n through cycles 5 to 10 of the energy, read at 100) and 5n's union (top 4 raw plus top 4 sqrt).
- H108 (ranks): Spearman(map prediction, DFN@100) above the sqrt fit's on at least 12 of 17 conditions. Refuted if on 8 or fewer.
- H109 (fade size): median over designs of |predicted &minus; DFN fade from cycle 10 to 100| below the sqrt fit's on at least 12 of 17. Refuted if on 8 or fewer.
- H110 (screen): the union of the top 4 raw and the top 4 by the map is within 1% of the pool's DFN best on every condition. Refuted if above 3% on any.
- H111 (against 5n): that union's gap is no larger than 5n's union's on every condition. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- Caveats fixed now: committed pools only (seen truth, unseen predictor); the map is open-circuit, so 5r's H107 says it should over-predict fade on designs far from open circuit (`NCA_Kim2011`); plating parameters are OKane2022's on six of seven sets; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Script: `experiments/mapscreen/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5r outcome: an electrode-balance map with no fitted constant turns each DFN run's own lithium loss into its energy fade within 0.05 on 34 of 40 fresh fading designs, ranks fade within a condition at least as well as the lithium share on all 5 fading conditions (`Ai2020@0.3x` +0.95 against +0.48), and errs where the design runs far from open circuit (Spearman &minus;0.73). H104 to H107 supported
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (8 conditions x designs 5 to 12 by j x {SEI + plating, neither}), 20 cycles, 670 s of solver time, about 13 minutes wall on 4 cores (3 workers). None failed; three no-degradation runs stopped early (`Ecker2015@1x` designs 5 and 6 at cycles 16 and 18, `Mohtat2020@1x` design 11 at cycle 8), so 61 designs were scored, 40 fading (lithium loss from cycle 2 to 20 above 3% of n_Li; all on `Ai2020`, `Chen2020`, `NCA_Kim2011` and `OKane2022`). The map costs about 0.05 s per design after a one-off 4 s per set for the OCP tables.
- H104 supported: |map &minus; DFN added fade| &le; 0.05 on 34 of 40 fading designs (0.85; bar 0.75). The six misses: four `NCA_Kim2011@1x` designs (over-predicted by 0.08 to 0.24) and two on `Ai2020@1x` (designs 8 and 10, over by 0.065 and 0.063). All six have E2 / E_ocv between 0.16 and 0.56; five are below 0.32.
- H105 supported: mean absolute error 0.038 against the proportional baseline's 0.053 (ratio 0.71; bar 0.75); medians 0.029 against 0.032. By condition the map wins clearly on `Ai2020` (0.3x: 0.012 against 0.049; 1x: 0.033 against 0.086), ties on `Chen2020@1x` (0.030 against 0.033) and `NCA_Kim2011@1x` (0.083 against 0.082), and loses on `OKane2022@1x` (0.032 against 0.017), where it under-predicts every design by a median 0.035; it under-predicts `Chen2020@1x` by a median 0.029.
- H106 supported: Spearman(map, DFN added fade) &ge; Spearman(LLI share, DFN added fade) on 5 of 5 fading conditions: `Ai2020@0.3x` +0.95 against +0.48, `Ai2020@1x` +0.81 against +0.21, `NCA_Kim2011@1x` +0.79 against +0.69, `OKane2022@1x` +0.38 against +0.36, `Chen2020@1x` +0.10 against +0.10 (a tie, which the plan counts).
- H107 supported: Spearman(E2 / E_ocv, |map error|) = &minus;0.73 over the 40 (bar &le; &minus;0.3). The worst misses are the designs DFN delivers least of their open-circuit energy: `NCA_Kim2011@1x` designs 7, 9 and 11 at E2 / E_ocv 0.19, 0.25 and 0.16, over-predicted by 0.24, 0.08 and 0.20. Such a design is rate-limited (salt or solid diffusion) at this current, so lithium it loses comes out of capacity it was not reaching anyway.
- Reading: the fade decomposes. On designs that run near open circuit (E2 / E_ocv above about 0.6), lost energy is a design-only function of lost lithium, and that function is the electrode balance: how far the negative's excess and the cut-offs let the window slide before it shrinks. That is why energy fade per unit of lithium varies twofold within a pool (5q) and why the lithium-loss rate alone does not rank it. On `Ai2020`, the fade-limited cell where 5o's front and 5q's predictor failed, the map ranks fade at +0.81 to +0.95. What it does not give: the lithium-loss rate (still simulated here, from DFN itself), and a rate term for designs far from open circuit, where a constant does as well or better. The consistent under-prediction on `OKane2022` and `Chen2020` (both graphite-SiO<sub>x</sub> against NMC811 in PyBaMM, CC-only protocol at E2 / E_ocv about 0.5 to 0.6) is the same rate effect in the other direction and is not explained here.
- Caveats: 20 cycles, not 100; the map is fed DFN's own lithium loss, so this is a decomposition test, not a predictor; plating parameters are OKane2022's on six of seven sets (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); open-circuit window only. The 40 fading designs come from 5 conditions, 8 each, so per-condition Spearman values carry wide intervals.
- Output: `experiments/balance/bench.jsonl`, `balance.jsonl`, `ocp.json`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `balance.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5r: does an electrode-balance map turn the lithium a DFN design loses into the energy it loses? (plan, written before the fresh bench's results were read; H104 to H107 are this entry's)
- Question: 5q found that energy lost per unit of lithium lost varies about threefold within one pool, so the lithium-loss rate alone cannot rank fade, and asked for an electrode-balance map. If such a map, with no simulation, turns each DFN run's own lithium loss into its energy fade, then the fade problem splits cleanly into a design-only map and a lithium-loss rate, and only the rate needs simulation.
- The map, fixed now (`experiments/balance/run.py`): per design, from the parameter set and the design mapping only, the negative and positive lithium capacities Q_n and Q_p (c_max &times; active fraction &times; thickness &times; area &times; parallel count, mol), the cyclable lithium n_Li (both particles' initial concentrations, which PyBaMM's `initial_soc` keeps) and the set's two OCP curves (401-point tables). The open-circuit window is where U_p(y) &minus; U_n(x) crosses the lower and upper cut-offs with Q_n x + Q_p y = n_Li; its energy is F Q_n &int; (U_p &minus; U_n) dx. Predicted fade from cycle 2 to 20 = 1 &minus; E(n_Li &minus; L<sub>18</sub>) / E(n_Li &minus; L<sub>0</sub>), with L<sub>k</sub> the lithium the DFN run (SEI + plating) had lost by the end of cycle k+1 (cycle k's discharge follows cycle k&minus;1's charge). No fitted constant. Baseline: proportional, k &times; LLI share with k = 1.64, the median over the dev bench's fading designs, fixed now. Target: DFN's degradation-added fade, fade(SEI + plating) &minus; fade(no degradation), as in 5p, so protocol drift is not charged to the map.
- Seen before this entry (development): 5p's committed bench (8 conditions &times; designs 1 to 4 by j, 31 scored, 20 fading, i.e. losing more than 3% of n_Li from cycle 2 to 20). There the map is within 0.05 of DFN's added fade on 17 of 20 fading designs (0.85), MAE 0.034 against the baseline's 0.059 (median 0.025 against 0.022), ranks fade within a condition at least as well as the lithium share on 5 of 5 fading conditions (Ai2020@1x +1.00 against &minus;0.60), and errs most where DFN's cycle-2 energy is furthest below the open-circuit energy (Spearman &minus;0.40; `NCA_Kim2011@1x`, MAE 0.09, E2/E_ocv down to 0.27). A variant whose window shrinks by an overpotential fitted to DFN's cycle-2 energy was worse on dev and is not tested.
- Fresh bench, fixed now: the same 8 conditions, the next 8 designs by j of each committed pool (positions 5 to 12), DFN for 20 cycles with SEI + plating and with neither (128 runs), 5p's mapping, current and protocol, one fresh process per run. Each run also stores per-cycle Ah and lithium in particles.
- H104 (the map is right): |map &minus; DFN added fade| &le; 0.05 on at least 75% of fresh fading designs. Refuted if under 50%.
- H105 (the balance matters): the map's mean absolute error is below 0.75 of the proportional baseline's on the fresh fading designs. Refuted if not below the baseline's.
- H106 (it ranks): within each fresh fading condition (at least 3 fading designs), Spearman(map, DFN added fade) &ge; Spearman(LLI share, DFN added fade). Supported if on every such condition; refuted if on half or fewer.
- H107 (where it fails): across fresh fading designs, Spearman(E2 / E_ocv, |map error|) &le; &minus;0.3, i.e. the map errs where the design runs far from open circuit. Refuted if &ge; 0.
- Caveats fixed now: 20 cycles, not 100; the map uses DFN's own lithium loss (it is a decomposition test, not yet a predictor); PyBaMM's plating parameters are OKane2022's on six of seven sets (5p); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); open circuit, so no rate term.
- Script: `experiments/balance/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5q outcome: two SPMe cycles that see lithium plating do not rank the 100-cycle DFN designs; the cycle-2 plating rate does not rank the fade (median Spearman 0.03 on the 24 unseen conditions), a self-limited plating correction makes the ranking worse than SPMe's plain cycle-2 energy on 20 of 24, and SPMe cannot run 456 of the 2,324 designs, the DFN best among them on 7 of 36 pools. H100 to H103 refuted
- Bench: 2,324 two-cycle SPMe runs with SEI and plating (every committed design of the 36 conditions 5b to 5o ran to 100 DFN cycles), 4 workers, about 35 minutes on 4 cores; 1,868 finished both cycles, 456 failed (SPMe's salt goes negative on high-current pools, as 5d found: 56 of 64 on `NCA_Kim2011@1.25x`, 42 on `Chen2020@2x`, 41 on `OKane2022@2x`). A predictor run cost a median 0.03 to 0.15 of a 100-cycle DFN run's time over a pool (median 0.06 on the unseen 24).
- H100 refuted: Spearman(p2, DFN fade from cycle 2 to 100) was below 0.3 on 19 of the 24 unseen conditions and at least 0.5 on none (median 0.03, range &minus;0.52 to +0.47). On the 12 seen ones it was 0.31 to 0.84.
- H101 refuted: the self-limited prediction ranked designs worse than SPMe's plain cycle-2 energy w2 on 20 of 24 unseen conditions (median Spearman 0.58 against 0.82). Carrying cycle 2's loss on unlimited (`linear`) predicts total loss on most designs (median Spearman &minus;0.08).
- H102 refuted: keeping the top half by the self-limited prediction (designs it could not run kept too) before the union screen missed the pool's DFN best by more than 1% on 5 of 24 and more than 3% on 2 (`Chen2020@1.75x` 6.1%, `Chen2020@1x` 3.6%). The plain union screen's worst is 0.23%.
- H103 refuted: that cut cost 0.92 to 1.45 of the union screen's wall time (median 1.15), above 0.8 on all 24: the predictor's own cost, charged on every design, is more than the half of the ten-cycle DFN runs it saves, and the designs it cannot run still go to the union.
- Amended after the verdicts, before this entry: the first report scored gaps against the best of the designs SPMe could run, which hid the 7 pools where SPMe fails on the DFN-best design. Gaps are now against the whole pool's DFN best, with designs whose predictor failed kept by the share cut and left out of top-K lists. No verdict changed (all four were refuted under both).
- Why it fails (5p's committed plating-only runs, post hoc): energy fade per unit of lithium lost over cycles 2 to 20 ranges from &minus;0.68 to 2.35 across the 20 designs that lost more than 3% of their lithium, and from 0.94 to 2.08 within one pool (`Ai2020@0.3x`). How much energy a design loses for its lost lithium depends on its electrode balance (which electrode limits capacity, and how much excess lithium the negative holds), so a lithium-loss rate alone cannot rank fade. The per-cycle plating loss also halves over 20 DFN cycles (5p), which the OCP-rise correction captures only weakly on graphite's flat plateau: it predicts a median fade of 0.79 to 0.82 on `Ai2020` 0.25x to 0.375x against 0.52 to 0.61 measured.
- Reading: the cheap fade predictor 5p asked for needs an electrode-balance map from lost lithium to lost capacity (PyBaMM's electrode-SOH solver is the natural tool; a scratch try on one `Ai2020` design returned non-monotone capacities, so it needs care), not a better plating rate. Until then, 5o's salt floor plus 5n's union screen stays the procedure: it is exact or within 0.32% on all 36 conditions, and nothing here is cheaper.
- Caveats: committed pools only; the 24 unseen conditions are mostly energy-decided, so H102 tested the cut's safety more than the fade regime; SPMe's plating, not DFN's; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 20% of designs have no SPMe prediction at all.
- Output: `experiments/plating/predict.jsonl`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `plating.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5q: can two SPMe cycles that see lithium plating predict the 100-cycle DFN fade, and cut the pool before any DFN run? (plan, written before the predictor's results on 24 of the 36 conditions were read; H100 to H103 are this entry's)
- Question: 5p showed every fade number in the programme is lithium plating, and 5o's zero-simulation (U, r_kt) front misses by up to 4.7% where fade decides (low-current `Ai2020`, best design on layers 4 to 7), because it ranks high energy first. 5p's next step asked for a near-zero-simulation plating predictor tested as a pool cut.
- The predictor, fixed now (`experiments/plating/run.py`): SPMe with the programme's options (solvent-diffusion SEI and irreversible plating, TR.OPTS), 5m's current mapping and CC protocol, two cycles. From it: w2, SPMe's cycle-2 Wh per kg of stack; r2, lithium lost during cycle 2 (mol; "Total lithium lost [mol]", which carries the parallel count, 5p); p2 = r2 over the initial inventory. Three predicted cycle-100 Wh/kg, none with a fitted constant: `w2`; `linear`, w2 &times; (1 &minus; 98 r2 F / Ah2); and `limited`, the same with the loss integrated under PyBaMM's own irreversible plating law, j &prop; exp(&minus;&alpha; F &Delta;&phi; / RT), with &Delta;&phi; raised by the negative OCP's rise as lost lithium leaves it emptier at the end of each charge (U(&theta;<sub>end</sub> &minus; L / Q<sub>n</sub>) &minus; U(&theta;<sub>end</sub>), from the run's end-of-charge stoichiometry and the set's OCP function).
- Seen before this entry (development, scratch): on the 11 `Ai2020` pools and `Chen2020@0.5x`, Spearman(p2, DFN fade from cycle 2 to 100) was +0.31 to +0.65; on `Ai2020` 0.25x to 0.375x Spearman with DFN@100 was w2 &minus;0.06 to +0.51, `limited` +0.13 to +0.35, and `limited` over-predicts the median fade (0.79 to 0.82 against 0.52 to 0.61). On 5p's committed bench, the energy fade per unit of lithium lost varies about threefold between designs of one pool (`Ai2020@0.3x`), so the lost lithium alone cannot rank them. The predictor's runs on the other 24 conditions (`Chen2020` 1x to 2x, `Ecker2015`, `Marquis2019`, `Mohtat2020`, `NCA_Kim2011`, `OKane2022`) had not been read.
- Procedures scored (no tuning): DFN@100 on the top K = 3, 4, 6, 8 by each predicted score; keep the top half (or quarter) by each, then 5n's union screen on them. The predictor's measured time is charged on every design.
- H100 (the cycle-2 rate ranks the fade): Spearman(p2, DFN fade 2 to 100) &ge; 0.5 on every unseen condition. Refuted if below 0.3 on any.
- H101 (plating improves the ranking): Spearman(`limited`, DFN@100) &ge; Spearman(w2, DFN@100) on every unseen condition. Refuted if lower on more than half.
- H102 (a cut that keeps the best): keep the top half by `limited`, then the union screen: gap &le; 1% on every unseen condition. Refuted if above 3% on any.
- H103 (and pays): its wall time &le; 0.6 of the plain union screen's on every unseen condition. Refuted if above 0.8 on any.
- Also reported, no verdicts: all 36 conditions; the seen 12 separately; 5p's energy fade per lithium lost, design by design.
- Caveats fixed now: committed pools only, no fresh bench; the 24 unseen conditions are mostly ones where energy already decides (the best sits on (U, r_kt) layer 1 to 3), the exceptions `Mohtat2020@3x` (layer 7) and `NCA_Kim2011@0.5x`, so H102 and H103 test safety more than the fade-limited regime; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); SPMe's plating, not DFN's.
- Script: `experiments/plating/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5p outcome: lithium plating makes the DFN designs fade, not SEI. Plating alone reproduces 0.998 to 1.015 of the degradation-added fade on the five conditions that fade, SEI alone 0.000 to 0.006; and PyBaMM's side-reaction lithium variables under-count by exactly the parallel-electrode count (34 on Ai2020)
- Bench: 128 DFN runs (8 committed conditions x the first 4 designs of each pool x {SEI + plating, plating, SEI, neither}), 20 cycles, 504 s of solver time, about 8 minutes wall on 4 cores (3 workers). One run stopped at cycle 10 (`Ecker2015@1x` design 2, no degradation), so that design was dropped: 31 designs scored.
- H96 supported: (Total lithium lost) / (Total lithium lost to side reactions) / (parallel-electrode count) = 1.00000 on all 93 degraded runs (34.0000 raw on every Ai2020 run, 1.0000 elsewhere).
- H97 supported: on the five conditions where degradation adds more than 1% fade from cycle 2 to 20 (`OKane2022@1x` 13.4%, `Ai2020@0.3x` 17.3%, `Ai2020@1x` 5.2%, `Chen2020@1x` 12.8%, `NCA_Kim2011@1x` 24.5%), plating alone reproduces a median 1.002, 0.999, 1.015, 1.000 and 0.998 of it.
- H98 supported: SEI alone's share is 0.006, 0.002, 0.000, 0.004 and &minus;0.002 on the same five. By cycle 20, SEI took 0.007% to 0.15% of the lithium inventory; plating took 7.8 to 13.2% on the four graphite conditions that fade and 33.7% on `NCA_Kim2011@1x`.
- H99 supported: the closed form L<sup>2</sup> = L0<sup>2</sup> + 2 V D c_sol t / z, times z a / V and the electrode volume (parallel count included), matches the SEI-only run's total lithium lost to a relative error under 1e-4 on all 31 runs (rounds to 0.0000).
- Also seen: `Ecker2015`, `Mohtat2020` and `Marquis2019` barely fade at 1x (degradation adds &minus;1.4% to +0.4%, plating 0.5 to 0.7% of inventory), so they were not scored. Without degradation the energy still drifts on `Ai2020@1x` (+1.6% fade) and `NCA_Kim2011@1x` (&minus;5.0%, energy rises), the CC-only protocol settling into its cycle; on the rest it is flat after cycle 2.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts): in the plating-only runs, lithium lost grows as about n<sup>0.83 to 0.90</sup> from cycle 5 to 20 on `OKane2022` and `Chen2020`, n<sup>0.57 to 0.83</sup> on `Ai2020@0.3x`, near linear on the three that barely fade, and n<sup>0.20 to 0.28</sup> on `NCA_Kim2011`, where plating saturates early.
- Reading: every fade number from 5b to 5o is lithium plating, under OKane2022's plating parameters, which 5b's copy rule gives every set but OKane2022. The sqrt(cycle) law that motivated 5n's fade fit is SEI's, and SEI contributes nothing measurable over 20 cycles; the fit worked on low-current `Ai2020` because plating there happens to grow close to sqrt(n) (exponent 0.57 to 0.83), and it was anti-correlated on `NCA_Kim2011` because plating there saturates (exponent 0.2 to 0.3). No committed score changes: scores are integrated voltage &times; current, and no milestone read PyBaMM's loss variables (energy-cell-dfn's diagnostics did, on OKane2022, whose parallel count is 1). What changes is the explanation and the next step: a cheap fade predictor has to predict plating (negative-electrode surface overpotential against Li/Li<sup>+</sup> during charge), not SEI; and because plating is a copied parameter set on six of seven cells, the programme's fade results are a statement about OKane2022's plating kinetics on those geometries, which the paper must say.
- PyBaMM: "Loss of lithium to {domain} lithium plating [mol]" (`base_plating.py`, Q = c L_k L_y L_z), the SEI counterparts and their sum "Total lithium lost to side reactions [mol]" omit "Number of electrodes connected in parallel to make a cell", which "Total lithium [mol]" includes, although the source comments say the two "should give same value". A ready-to-file report is `experiments/attribution/UPSTREAM.md` (filing it is an owner step).
- Caveats: 31 designs, 20 cycles, the first four of each committed pool; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); PyBaMM's submodels, not a measured cell; the 100-cycle shares are not measured here.
- Output: `experiments/attribution/bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `attribution.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5p: what makes the DFN designs fade? An ablation of PyBaMM's SEI and plating options, and a check of its lithium bookkeeping (plan, written before the bench; H96 to H99 are this entry's)
- Question: 5n justified its sqrt(cycle) fade fit by SEI growth ("the first fade term in PyBaMM's degradation model"), and 5o's next step asked for a cheaper fade predictor. Every milestone since 5b runs DFN with {"SEI": "solvent-diffusion limited", "lithium plating": "irreversible"}. Which of the two makes the designs fade has never been measured.
- Seen before this entry (scratch, not committed; PyBaMM 26.10.1.1): (1) PyBaMM's own solvent-diffusion law (`sei_growth.py`: j_sei = &minus;F c_sol D / L) integrates to L(t)<sup>2</sup> = L0<sup>2</sup> + 2 V D c_sol t / z, independent of potential and current. On the 2,132 development designs its per-condition median is under 0.5% of capacity over 100 cycles on every set but NCA_Kim2011 (1.8 to 3.9%), about 0.2 to 0.5% on Ai2020, against 55 to 65% energy fade at cycle 100 in the Ai2020 benches. (2) On one Ai2020@0.3x design over 3 DFN cycles, "Total lithium lost [mol]" was 34.0 times "Total lithium lost to side reactions [mol]"; with SEI alone the particles lost 0.009% of their lithium, with plating alone 2.6%, with neither 0. (3) On default Ai2020 (34 parallel electrodes) and OKane2022 (1, then set to 5) the ratio equals the parallel-electrode count to four decimals: the "Loss of lithium to ... [mol]" variables multiply by electrode height &times; width (`base_plating.py`, Q = c L_k L_y L_z) but not by the parallel count, which "Total lithium [mol]" includes. No milestone score reads those variables (scores are trapezoid Wh from voltage and current), and `energy-cell-dfn`'s diagnostics read them on OKane2022 only (one electrode), so no committed number changes.
- The procedure, fixed now (`experiments/attribution/run.py`): 8 committed conditions, one per parameter set at 1x plus Ai2020@0.3x (OKane2022@1x, Ai2020@0.3x, Ai2020@1x, Chen2020@1x, Ecker2015@1x, NCA_Kim2011@1x, Mohtat2020@1x, Marquis2019@1x); the first 4 designs (by j) of each committed pool; DFN for 20 cycles under four option sets (both, SEI only, plating only, none), mapping, current and protocol 5g's, unchanged. Fade is 1 &minus; Wh(cycle 20) / Wh(cycle 2) (cycle 1 starts from the set's own initial state, which a CC-only charge does not return to). Degradation-added fade = fade(both) &minus; fade(none); each option's share = (fade(option) &minus; fade(none)) / added. A design counts only if all four runs finish 20 cycles; condition values are medians over its designs; H97 and H98 score conditions where degradation adds more than 1% fade.
- H96 (bookkeeping): on every degraded run, (Total lithium lost) / (Total lithium lost to side reactions) / (parallel-electrode count) is within 1e-3 of 1. Refuted if any differs by more than 1e-2.
- H97 (plating drives the fade): plating alone reproduces at least 0.9 of the degradation-added fade on every scored condition. Refuted if below 0.7 on any.
- H98 (SEI does not): |SEI alone's share| is below 0.1 on every scored condition. Refuted if above 0.3 on any.
- H99 (the closed form is PyBaMM's SEI): the closed-form SEI lithium at the SEI-only run's elapsed time is within 10% of that run's total lithium lost, on every run. Refuted if off by more than 25% on any.
- Caveats fixed now: 32 designs over 8 conditions, 20 cycles (not 100); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); the ablation measures PyBaMM's submodels, not a measured cell; sets that lack plating and SEI parameters carry OKane2022's (5b's copy rule), so "plating drives the fade" is a statement about those copied parameters on every set but OKane2022.
- Amended after the bench started, before any result was read: the first bench ran the 128 runs in a process pool, and a worker died abruptly after 24 runs (pool broken; those 24 rows were kept). The rest ran as one fresh process per run (as 5b to 5o do), so a crash fails one run, not the bench. No run failed that way.
- Script: `experiments/attribution/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5o outcome: a closed-form salt floor (drop designs with r_kt &lt; &minus;0.2 before any DFN run) kept the DFN-best design on all three fresh pools and cut 5n's union screen to 0.48 to 0.79 of its time (0.15 to 0.20 of all-DFN); the plain (U, r_kt) front cut stays rejected, because where fade decides the best design sits on layers 4 to 7
- Bench: 192 runs (64 Sobol seed-13 designs x one chunked, resumed DFN run to 100 cycles x NCA_Kim2011@0.875x, Chen2020@1.75x, Ai2020@0.875x), 3 workers, about 20 minutes on 4 cores, none failed or dropped. r_kt cost a median 0.042 to 0.044 s per design, as in development.
- H92 supported: the floor screen's answer is the pool's DFN@100 best on all three (gap 0, 0, 0; bar 1%).
- H93 supported: the best design's r_kt is +0.31, &minus;0.04 and +0.36, all above &minus;0.2; the floor dropped 35, 26 and 25 of 64 designs (lowest r_kt &minus;1.54, &minus;1.00, &minus;2.57).
- H94 supported: wall time 0.57, 0.48 and 0.79 of the plain union screen's (bar 0.80), or 0.152, 0.158 and 0.197 of all-DFN@100 against the union's 0.266, 0.328 and 0.249.
- H95 supported: the floor screen's gap equals the union's on all three (both exact).
- Also seen: a floor at 0 would have missed by 9.3% on Chen2020@1.75x, where the best design's r_kt is &minus;0.04, so the floor has to sit below 0 (as development said: the best design sits at or just past the salt edge on high-current pools). The 3-layer front cut was exact on all three here (the best sat on layer 1 or 2), at 0.15 to 0.27; 5m's ladder was exact at 0.22 to 0.26; 5k's front with DFN@100 missed by 1.4% on NCA_Kim2011@0.875x.
- Over all 36 conditions seen (33 development, 3 fresh): the floor screen is exact on 32, worst 0.32%, the same answers as the union on every one; it costs 0.41 to 1.03 of the union's time (above 1 only where no design is below the floor, by the 0.04 s per design r_kt takes).
- Reading: a design search does not need a simulation to discard designs that will badly starve their electrolyte. 5i's closed-form salt number is cheap (0.04 s against about 7 to 25 s for a 100-cycle DFN run) and the best design never sat below r_kt &minus;0.08 on 36 pools, while up to 89% of a high-current pool does. The floor's saving is largest exactly where the union screen was dearest on high-current pools (half its time on Chen2020@1.75x) and zero on the low-current Ai2020 pools, where nothing is starved and fade decides; those stay at 0.25 to 0.31 of all-DFN, and need a cheaper fade predictor, not a salt one. The (U, r_kt) front is the wrong zero-simulation cut for that reason: it ranks high energy first, and on fade-limited cells high energy fades fastest.
- Caveats: three fresh conditions, all at currents where the floor bites and none in the low-current fade regime (so H92 tested the floor's safety, not the union's hard cases); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine; the floor was picked on development data; r_kt is SPMe's closed-form salt minimum, not DFN's; fade is PyBaMM's SEI and plating submodels.
- Output: `experiments/frontcut/urkt.jsonl`, `rkt_time.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `frontcut.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5o: can the zero-simulation numbers cut the pool before any DFN run? Not the (U, r_kt) front, which drops the best design where fade decides; a salt floor (r_kt &ge; &minus;0.2) before 5n's union screen instead (plan, written before the fresh bench; H92 to H95 are this entry's)
- Question: 5n's union screen cannot cost less than ten DFN cycles of every design (0.15 to 0.17 of a 100-cycle run each), and its next step asked for a zero-simulation first cut, naming 5k's (U, r_kt) Pareto front. U (thermodynamic Wh/kg over stack mass) and r_kt (5i's closed-form minimum salt ratio) need parameter values only.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/frontcut/dev.txt`, all 33 conditions of 5b to 5n; U and r_kt recomputed for all 2,132 designs, `urkt.jsonl`, and identical to 5l's stored values on its 192): the front cut fails where it would pay. On Ai2020 at 0.25x to 0.75x and Mohtat2020@3x the pool's DFN@100 best sits on (U, r_kt) layer 4 to 7, at U percentile 0.08 to 0.31, because the high-energy designs that dominate it fade fastest. Keeping 3 layers before the union misses by up to 4.7% (exact on 24 of 33), 6 layers by up to 1.3% (28 of 33); a two-corner front (layers of (U, r_kt) and of (&minus;U, r_kt)) is no better (up to 4.7%). What does hold: the best design's r_kt was &minus;0.08 or higher on every one of the 33 conditions (lowest OKane2022@1.75x &minus;0.08, NCA_Kim2011@1.25x &minus;0.06), while up to 89% of a high-current pool sits below &minus;0.1. A salt floor dropping designs with r_kt below &minus;0.5, &minus;0.3, &minus;0.2, &minus;0.15 or &minus;0.1 keeps the union's exact record (29 of 33, worst 0.32%), and a floor at 0 does not (worst 10.7%). At &minus;0.2 it costs 0.41 to 1.03 of the union's wall time (median 0.97; r_kt measured at 0.043 s per design, `rkt_time.json`, charged on every design): it saves 31 to 59% on the high-current OKane2022, NCA_Kim2011 and Chen2020 pools and costs up to 3% more where nothing is starved. &minus;0.2 was picked as the cheapest floor with a margin of at least 0.1 below the lowest best seen, so it is fitted, and the fresh conditions are its test.
- The procedure, fixed now (`experiments/frontcut/run.py`, `floor_union`, TAU = &minus;0.2): compute r_kt for every design; drop designs with r_kt &lt; &minus;0.2; on the rest run 5n's union screen unchanged (DFN to cycle 10; resume to 100 the top 4 by cycle-10 Wh/kg and the top 4 by the sqrt(cycle) extrapolation of cycles 5 to 10; answer the best DFN@100 among them). Wall time: the measured r_kt time on every design plus the kept designs' measured segments as 5n charges them.
- Fresh conditions, fixed before any run and chosen only by the floor's predicted share on the new designs (zero simulation): NCA_Kim2011@0.875x (0.55 of designs below the floor), Chen2020@1.75x (0.41) and Ai2020@0.875x (0.39), currents no milestone ran. 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 13 (not used before); one DFN run per design in 10-cycle chunks to 100, each resumed from the last (5m's `_child`, unchanged), 3 at a time; designs that fail before cycle 100 are dropped as in 5m and 5n. A condition is kept if its DFN probe passes (`probe.json`).
- H92 (answer): the floor screen's answer is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every fresh condition. Refuted if more than 3% below on any.
- H93 (the floor is safe): the pool's DFN@100 best has r_kt &ge; &minus;0.2 on every fresh condition. Refuted if below on any.
- H94 (it pays where it bites): its wall time is at most 0.80 of the plain union screen's on every fresh condition. Refuted if above 0.95 on any.
- H95 (no worse than the union): its gap minus the union's gap is at most 0 on every fresh condition. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the 3-layer front cut, floors from &minus;0.5 to 0, 5m's ladder, 5k's front with DFN@100, the best design's (U, r_kt) layer and U percentile.
- Caveats fixed now: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; the floor was picked on development data; r_kt is SPMe's closed-form salt minimum, not DFN's; fade is PyBaMM's SEI and plating submodels.
- Script: `experiments/frontcut/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5n outcome: ten DFN cycles plus a sqrt(cycle) fade fit find the best design to within 0.16% on three fresh pools, where the raw 10-cycle screen missed by 9.2% and 3.9%; the extrapolation lifts the low-current Ai2020 ranking from Spearman 0.10 and 0.48 to 0.57 and 0.63, but the screen costs 0.24 to 0.32 of all-DFN, not under 0.30
- Bench: 192 runs (64 Sobol seed-12 designs x one chunked, resumed DFN run to 100 cycles x Ai2020@0.275x, Ai2020@0.35x, NCA_Kim2011@1.25x), 3 workers, about 45 minutes on 4 cores, none killed. On Ai2020@0.275x 13 of 64 designs failed in the first cycle (IDA error-test failures, as at 0.25x and 0.3x) and 5 on Ai2020@0.35x; they were dropped. The probe keep rule was amended before the bench (`probe.json`, `kept_note`): SPMe, which this milestone never runs, failed at NCA_Kim2011@1.25x and DFN passed.
- H87 supported: the union screen's answer is 0.16% short of the pool's DFN best on Ai2020@0.275x and exact on Ai2020@0.35x and NCA_Kim2011@1.25x (bar 1%). 5l's fixed DFN@10 top-3 screen missed by 9.2% and 3.9% on the two Ai2020 pools.
- H88 supported: Spearman(extrapolated, DFN@100) 0.57 against the raw cycle-10 energy's 0.10 on Ai2020@0.275x, and 0.63 against 0.48 on Ai2020@0.35x. On NCA_Kim2011@1.25x it is -0.68 against 0.97 (the raw list carried that pool, as planned; the sqrt top 3 alone missed by 52%).
- H89 mixed: wall time 0.315, 0.286 and 0.239 of all-DFN@100 (bar 0.30, refutation 0.40). The first 10 cycles cost a median 0.15 to 0.17 of a 100-cycle run, as 5m measured, so 64 designs to cycle 10 plus 8 to 100 lands near 0.3 on the slow Ai2020 runs.
- H90 mixed: gap minus 5m's ladder's gap +0.0016 (Ai2020@0.275x, where the ladder was exact), -0.0039 (Ai2020@0.35x, where the ladder missed by 0.39%) and 0. The ladder cost 0.294, 0.265 and 0.207, less than the union on every pool.
- H91 supported: raw cycle-10 Spearman 0.10 and 0.48 on the two Ai2020 pools, so both sit in the hard regime.
- Where the best sat: on Ai2020@0.35x it ranked 22nd by raw cycle-10 energy and 3rd by the extrapolation, the case the extrapolation was meant to catch. On Ai2020@0.275x it ranked 8th on both lists, so neither top 4 held it, and the 0.16% miss is the next design. On NCA_Kim2011@1.25x it ranked 1st raw and 58th extrapolated.
- Over all 33 conditions seen (30 development, 3 fresh): the union is exact on 29, worst 0.32%; the ladder on 30, worst 2.7%; the fixed DFN@10 screen on 24, worst 9.2%.
- Reading: on these cells a sqrt(cycle) line through cycles 5 to 10 does carry information about late fade that the raw energy lacks, and only where it matters: it helps on low-current Ai2020 and is anti-correlated on NCA_Kim2011, whose fade curves are not sqrt-shaped over 100 cycles. Keeping both lists makes the screen robust on every pool seen so far (worst 0.32%), at about the ladder's price (both 0.2 to 0.3 of all-DFN). The two are cheap in different places: the ladder does more DFN at cycles 20 to 50, the union at cycle 100. Neither gets below about 0.25 on slow Ai2020 runs, because the first ten cycles of every design already cost 0.15 to 0.17. To go lower, a screen has to run fewer than all designs to cycle 10, for example 5k's zero-simulation (U, r_kt) front as the first cut.
- Caveats: three conditions (51, 59 and 64 designs after drops); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; form, window and M were picked on development data; the sqrt law is PyBaMM's SEI submodel's, not a measured cell's.
- Output: `experiments/latefade/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `latefade.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5n: can the first ten DFN cycles predict late fade? A sqrt(cycle) fit over cycles 5 to 10, extrapolated to 100, beside the raw cycle-10 energy (plan, written before the fresh bench; H87 to H91 are this entry's)
- Question: 5m's ladder was 2.7% short on Ai2020@0.3x because the best design ranked 28th at cycle 10 and 1st only at cycle 50; its next step asked for a predictor of late fade from early quantities. PyBaMM's SEI submodel, the first fade term on these cells, grows the SEI roughly as the square root of time, so the cheapest predictor is the shape of the first ten cycles: a least-squares line in sqrt(n) through cycles 5 to 10 (after the first-cycle transient), read at n = 100. 5l tried a sqrt line through cycles 2 to 10 alone and found it ranked worse than the raw energy on its fresh pools; this milestone tests it as a second list beside the raw one, not as a replacement.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/latefade/dev.txt`, all 30 conditions of 5b to 5m; 5b to 5l charged k/100 of each DFN@100 run, 5m's three at measured segments): the sqrt extrapolation ranks the 100-cycle score better than the raw cycle-10 energy on all four low-current Ai2020 pools (0.25x 0.65 against 0.08, 0.3x 0.76 against 0.24, 0.375x and 0.4375x likewise), but much worse on NCA_Kim2011 (as low as -0.60 at 1x) and Chen2020, so alone it is exact on 17 of 30 (worst 25.9%). The union of the top 4 by raw cycle-10 Wh/kg and the top 4 by the extrapolation, resumed to cycle 100 (4 to 8 designs), is exact on 27 of 30, worst 0.32% (Ai2020@0.3x, where 5m's ladder missed by 2.7%), then 0.23% (NCA_Kim2011@1x) and 0.03% (Ai2020@0.375x), at a median 0.198 of all-DFN@100's wall time (max 0.301, Ai2020@0.3x measured). 5m's ladder on the same 30: 28 exact, worst 2.7%, median 0.195. About two dozen variants were tried on this data (scratch, not committed): K 10 and 20, fit windows from cycle 2 or K/2, sqrt, log, power-law and linear forms, m 2 to 4; the power-law and linear forms rank worse than raw everywhere, and the chosen one is the cheapest with a worst gap under 1%. So its development record is fitted, and the fresh conditions are its test.
- The procedure, fixed now (`experiments/latefade/run.py`, `union_screen`, K = 10, LO = 5, M = 4): DFN to cycle 10 on every design; fit each design's Wh/kg over cycles 5 to 10 with a line in sqrt(n) and read it at 100; resume to cycle 100 the top 4 by cycle-10 Wh/kg and the top 4 by that extrapolation; answer the best DFN@100 among them. Wall time: every design's measured first segment plus the resumed designs' remaining segments.
- Fresh conditions, fixed before any run: Ai2020@0.275x and Ai2020@0.35x (currents no milestone ran, inside the regime where every rank cut at cycle 10 missed), and NCA_Kim2011@1.25x (a current no milestone ran, on the cell where the extrapolation alone ranks worst, so the union's raw list is what has to carry it). 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 12 (not used before); one DFN run per design solved in 10-cycle chunks to cycle 100, each resumed from the last (5m's `_child`, unchanged; 5m showed resuming is bit-exact), one fresh process per design, 3 at a time. A condition is kept if its baseline probe passes (`probe.json`). Amended after the probe and before any bench run: the probe also solved SPMe, which this milestone never uses, and SPMe failed at NCA_Kim2011@1.25x (infeasible at initial conditions) while DFN passed; since only DFN is run here, a condition is kept when its DFN probe passes, so all three stay.
- H87 (answer): the union screen's answer is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every fresh condition. Refuted if more than 3% below on any.
- H88 (the predictor sees late fade): Spearman(sqrt-extrapolated cycle-100 Wh/kg, DFN@100) exceeds Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100) on both fresh Ai2020 conditions. Refuted if it is no higher on either; mixed otherwise.
- H89 (price): its wall time is at most 0.30 of all-DFN@100's on every fresh condition. Refuted if above 0.40 on any.
- H90 (against 5m's ladder): its gap is no larger than the ladder's on every fresh condition. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- H91 (the fresh Ai2020 currents are in the hard regime, so the test means something): Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100) is below 0.85 on at least one fresh Ai2020 condition. Refuted if at least 0.92 on both; mixed otherwise.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the log-form union, the sqrt top 3 alone, 5l's fixed DFN@10 top 3, cycle-20 Spearman, median segment times.
- Caveats fixed now: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; the form, window and M were picked on development data that includes the conditions they were picked to fix; PyBaMM's SEI and plating submodels set every fade number, so the sqrt law is the model's, not a measured cell's.
- Script: `experiments/latefade/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5m outcome: a successive-halving ladder over resumed DFN runs fixes most of the low-current misses (exact on 2 of 3 fresh pools, 2.7% short on Ai2020@0.3x against the 10-cycle screen's 8.8%) but costs 0.24 to 0.29 of all-DFN, not under 0.25; at Ai2020@0.3x the best design ranks 28th of 53 at cycle 10 and 1st only at cycle 50
- Bench: 216 runs (64 Sobol seed-11 designs x one chunked, resumed DFN run to 100 cycles, plus 8 uninterrupted DFN@100 runs per condition, x Ai2020@0.3x, Ai2020@0.4375x, Ecker2015@0.75x), 3 workers; 3 uninterrupted runs killed for memory and re-run one at a time. On Ai2020@0.3x 11 of 64 designs failed in the first cycle (IDA error-test failures, as 13 did at 0.25x in 5h) and were dropped; none elsewhere.
- H82 mixed: the ladder's answer is the pool's DFN best on Ai2020@0.4375x and Ecker2015@0.75x and 2.71% short on Ai2020@0.3x (bar 1%, refutation 3%).
- H83 mixed: wall time over all-DFN@100 0.286, 0.254 and 0.244 (bar 0.25, refutation 0.35). Development's k/100 charge was too low: the first 10 cycles take 0.15 to 0.17 of a 100-cycle run (setup and the first cycle), not 0.10; the same under-charge 5l found for separate DFN@10 runs.
- H84 supported: gap minus the fixed DFN@10 top-3 screen's gap -0.061, -0.013 and 0 (the fixed screen missed by 8.8% and 1.3% on the two Ai2020 pools).
- H85 supported: Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100) 0.24 on Ai2020@0.3x and 0.76 on Ai2020@0.4375x; so both fresh Ai2020 pools sit in the hard regime. At cycle 20 they are 0.48 and 0.89, at cycle 50 0.91 and 0.98.
- H86 supported: resumed and uninterrupted runs agree to a relative 0 (bit-identical per-cycle energies) on 7, 8 and 8 checked designs; the resumed run is also cheaper (median 0.97, 0.85 and 0.92 of the uninterrupted run's time), so chunking costs nothing.
- Post hoc (`posthoc.txt`, after the verdicts): on Ai2020@0.3x the best design (3) ranks 28th at cycle 10, 23rd at 20 and 1st at 50. No ladder that cuts below about 28 designs at cycle 10 keeps it: keeping 24 at cycle 10, 12 at cycle 20 or 5 at cycle 50 each still miss by 2.7%; keeping 32 then 16 then 4 misses by 0.9% at 0.38 of all-DFN; DFN@50 on all, top 3 to 100, is exact at 0.64. Also: the 5l top-3 stability signal (cycle 5 against 10) said "changing" on all three pools here, including Ecker2015 where every screen was exact, so it does not separate the regimes in either direction.
