- Reading: below about 0.4x on Ai2020 the order of the 100-cycle score is not set until somewhere between cycles 20 and 50, because the designs that start best fade fastest (the green curves cross the orange one after cycle 20 in `halving.svg`). A rank-based early cut cannot see that crossing; it has to keep the designs long enough to watch it, which on this cell means half the run. Elsewhere (27 of 30 conditions seen so far) the ladder is exact or within 0.25% at about a quarter of all-DFN's time, and resuming makes every rung free of restart cost. What is missing is a predictor of late fade from early quantities (fade rate at cycles 10 to 20, SEI thickness, lithium inventory), tested where fade-curve crossings happen.
- Caveats: three conditions, 64 designs each (53 on Ai2020@0.3x); one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine; the ladder was picked on development data; degradation is PyBaMM's SEI and plating submodels, not measured fade.
- Output: `experiments/halving/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `halving.svg`.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 5m: where fade keeps growing after cycle 10, does a successive-halving ladder over resumed DFN runs fix 5l's misses? (plan, written before the fresh bench; H82 to H86 are this entry's)
- Question: 5l's screen (DFN@10 on every design, DFN@100 on the top 3) was exact on its three fresh pools, but in development it missed by 7.2% and 6.0% on Ai2020 at 0.25x and 0.375x, where the cycle-10 ranking falls to Spearman 0.08 and 0.58 against the 100-cycle score. Its next step asked for an adaptive K and for the price of resuming a run instead of restarting it.
- Resuming, checked before this plan (one design, Ai2020@0.3x, baseline): solving 10 cycles, then 10 more with `Simulation.solve(starting_solution=...)`, gives the uninterrupted 20-cycle run's per-cycle energies to a relative 0 (same model object) or 6e-6 (a new Simulation), and the two segments' times add to the uninterrupted run's (4.5 s + 3.9 s against 8.4 s). So a short run that a screen keeps can be continued, not restarted.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/halving/dev.txt`, all 27 conditions of 5b to 5l, resumed runs charged k/100 of each DFN@100 run's time): a stopping signal does not work. On Ai2020@0.25x the top-3 set at cycle 10 equals the one at cycle 5 and Spearman(cycle 5, cycle 10) is 0.94, yet cycle 10 ranks the 100-cycle score at 0.08; an "extend while the top 3 changes" rule (10, 20, 50) kept the 7.2% miss. A schedule with no signal does: DFN to cycle 10 on all, the top 16 resumed to 20, the top 8 to 50, the top 3 to 100 is exact on 26 of 27 (worst 0.23%, NCA_Kim2011@1x) at a median 0.194 of all-DFN@100's time (max 0.216). The fixed screens, also resumed: DFN@10 top 3 exact on 22 (worst 7.2%, 0.15 of the time), DFN@20 top 3 on 24 (worst 2.3%, Ecker2015@0.5x, 0.24), 10->16, 20->3 on 24 (worst 2.3%, 0.17). About a dozen ladders were tried on this data (scratch, not committed: keep counts 8 to 20, rungs 20 to 50); the chosen one is the cheapest seen that is exact on Ai2020@0.25x, 0.375x and Ecker2015@0.5x, so its development record is fitted, and the fresh conditions are its test.
- The procedure, fixed now (`experiments/halving/run.py`, `halving`, PLAN (10, 16), (20, 8), (50, 3)): DFN to cycle 10 on every design; keep the top 16 by cycle-10 Wh/kg and resume them to 20; keep the top 8 and resume them to 50; keep the top 3 and resume them to 100; answer the best DFN@100 among those 3. Wall time: each design's measured segments up to the rung it reached (setup included in the first). Compared with 5l's fixed screen (charged both resumed and as 5l charged it) and all-DFN@100.
- Fresh conditions, fixed before any run: Ai2020@0.3x and Ai2020@0.4375x (currents no milestone ran, inside the low-current regime where 5l's screen missed and between it and 0.5x), and Ecker2015@0.75x (between 0.5x, where every fixed screen missed by 2.3%, and 1x). 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 11 (not used before); one DFN run per design solved in segments to cycles 10, 20, 50 and 100, each resumed from the last, one fresh process per design, 3 at a time (amended before any fresh result was read: the first bench resumed from the whole solution, which doubled memory and had one run killed at 13 of 15 GB, so it was stopped after 2 designs, deleted, and restarted with 10-cycle chunks on one Simulation object, each resumed from the previous chunk's `last_state`; on the baseline design this reproduces the uninterrupted run's cycle-20 energy exactly); designs 0 to 7 also get an uninterrupted DFN@100 run (resume check). A condition is kept if its baseline probe passes (`probe.json`). Simulation, parameters and degradation options imported unchanged from 5g.
- H82 (answer): the ladder's answer is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every fresh condition. Refuted if more than 3% below on any.
- H83 (price): its wall time is at most 0.25 of all-DFN@100's on every fresh condition. Refuted if above 0.35 on any.
- H84 (against 5l's screen): its gap is no larger than the fixed DFN@10 top-3 screen's on every fresh condition. Refuted if larger by more than 1 point on any.
- H85 (the fresh Ai2020 currents are in the hard regime, so the test means something): Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100) is below 0.85 on at least one of the two Ai2020 conditions. Refuted if at least 0.92 on both; mixed otherwise.
- H86 (resuming is exact): on the 8 checked designs per condition, the resumed run's per-cycle energies match the uninterrupted run's to a relative 1e-4. Refuted if any differs by more than 1e-2.
- Also reported, no verdicts: Spearman of cycles 5, 10, 20 and 50 with the 100-cycle score, the top-3 stability signal, DFN@20 top 3, the two-rung ladder, median segment times, resumed against uninterrupted wall time.
- Caveats fixed now: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; the ladder was picked on development data that includes the conditions it was picked to fix; PyBaMM's SEI and plating submodels set every fade number.
- Script: `experiments/halving/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5l outcome: retention needs ten cycles of DFN, not a hundred. On three fresh conditions the cycle-10 energy ranks the 100-cycle score at Spearman 0.92 to 0.97, and the screen (DFN@10 on all, DFN@100 on the top 3) found the pool's DFN best exactly on all three at 0.19 of all-DFN's wall time
- Bench: 384 runs (64 Sobol seed-10 designs x DFN@100 and a separate DFN@10 x Ai2020@0.625x, NCA_Kim2011@0.75x, OKane2022@1.75x), 3 workers, about 28 minutes on 4 cores, none failed or killed, none dropped. A separate DFN@10 run reproduces the first 10 cycles of the DFN@100 run exactly (max relative difference 0 on all 192 pairs), so a search could stop a run at cycle 10 and resume it.
- H78 supported: Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100 Wh/kg) 0.97 (Ai2020@0.625x), 0.95 (NCA_Kim2011@0.75x), 0.92 (OKane2022@1.75x).
- H79 supported: the screen's answer is the pool's DFN best on all three (gap 0).
- H80 supported: its measured wall time is 0.198, 0.192 and 0.187 of all-DFN@100's. A separate DFN@10 run costs 0.14 to 0.15 of a DFN@100 run, not 0.10 (solver setup and the first cycle; development's 10/100 charge was too low, and the margin to 0.25 absorbed it).
- H81 supported: the screen's gap (0, 0, 0) is no larger than 5k's (U, r_kt) front's (0, 1.13% and 0.16%, at 0.16 to 0.27 of all-DFN's time).
- Also reported: the sqrt-cycle extrapolation of cycles 2 to 10 ranks worse than the raw cycle-10 energy on all three (+0.40, -0.56, +0.55): the first ten cycles fix the order, not the shape of the fade curve. Cycle-10 retention ranks 100-cycle retention at 0.84 to 0.94. Top 1 alone is exact on two of three (0.7% short on NCA_Kim2011@0.75x) at 0.15 to 0.16 of all-DFN; DFN@10 on the first two (U, r_kt) layers only is exact on all three at 0.11 to 0.12.
- Reading: of the three factors 5k split the score into, two need no simulation and the third, retention, is set mostly in the first ten cycles on these cells, so a 10-cycle DFN run carries nearly the whole 100-cycle ranking. Combined with 5k's front, the cheapest exact procedure seen on the fresh pools is DFN@10 on the first two (U, r_kt) layers and DFN@100 on the top 3 (0.11 to 0.12 of all-DFN). The development exceptions stand: on Ai2020 at 0.25x and 0.375x, where fade keeps growing after cycle 10, the cycle-10 ranking falls to 0.08 and 0.58 and the screen missed by 7.2% and 6.0%; the fresh Ai2020 rate (0.625x) sits above that regime, so the low-current failure was not re-tested here.
- Caveats: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; K = 10 and m = 3 were chosen on development data; PyBaMM's degradation submodels and parameter sets, not measured fade, set every retention number here.
- Output: `experiments/fade/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `fade.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5l: retention, the factor 5k left to DFN, read off a 10-cycle DFN run? (plan, written before the fresh bench; H78 to H81 are this entry's)
- Question: 5k split the DFN@100 score into U x utilisation x retention and found the first two need no simulation; retention is what made its zero-SPMe (U, r_kt) front miss by up to 4.7% (5.9% in development), and on Ai2020 it is the binding limit. Its next step asked for a retention predictor. A DFN run's Wh/kg at cycle K is U x utilisation x the retention to cycle K, so the cheapest candidate is a short DFN run. The screen tested here, fixed now (`experiments/fade/run.py`, `screen`): DFN@10 on every design, DFN@100 on the top 3 by cycle-10 Wh/kg, answer the best DFN@100 among them. Wall time is charged with separate DFN@10 runs (measured, setup included) plus the top 3 designs' own DFN@100 runs; continuing a run from cycle 10 is not assumed.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/fade/dev.txt`, all 24 conditions of 5b to 5k, DFN@10 read as the first 10 cycles of each DFN@100 run and charged 10/100 of its time): Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100 score) median 0.956, at least 0.85 on 22 of 24; the two exceptions are Ai2020 at its lowest currents (0.25x 0.08, 0.375x 0.58), where fade keeps growing after cycle 10. The screen finds the pool's DFN best exactly on 19 of 24 (5k's front: 9 of 24), median gap 0, worst 7.2% (Ai2020@0.25x; front 5.7%), at 0.13 to 0.16 of all-DFN@100's wall time (front 0.13 to 0.29). It is worse than the front by more than 3% once (Ai2020@0.375x, 6.0% against 2.7%). A least-squares line in sqrt(cycle) through cycles 2 to 10, extrapolated to cycle 100, ranks worse than the raw cycle-10 energy (median 0.84, as low as -0.84 on NCA_Kim2011@1x): the first ten cycles do not fix the shape of the fade curve, but they do fix most of the ordering. Not tried: a closed-form fade proxy from the SEI and cracking parameters.
- Fresh conditions, fixed before any run: the two cells 5k named, where energy above the flag does not decide, at currents no milestone ran (Ai2020@0.625x, between development's 2.4% miss at 0.5x and exact at 0.75x; NCA_Kim2011@0.75x), and OKane2022@1.75x (its best sat on the flag in 5j). All three passed the baseline probe (`experiments/fade/probe.json`). Designs: 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 10 (not used before); DFN@100 and a separate DFN@10 on each, one fresh process per run, 3 at a time. Simulation, mapping and pools imported unchanged from 5g and 5k.
- H78 (the short run orders the long one): Spearman(cycle-10 Wh/kg, DFN@100 Wh/kg) is at least 0.85 on every fresh condition. Refuted if below 0.5 on any.
- H79 (the screen's answer): the screen's answer is within 3% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every fresh condition. Refuted if more than 7.5% below on any.
- H80 (its price, measured): the screen's wall time is at most 0.25 of all-DFN@100's on every fresh condition. Refuted if above 0.40 on any.
- H81 (against 5k's front): the screen's gap is no larger than the (U, r_kt) front's (DFN@100 on layer 1) on every fresh condition. Refuted if larger by more than 3 points on any.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the sqrt-cycle extrapolation, top 1 and top 5, DFN@10 restricted to the first two and three (U, r_kt) layers, the measured DFN@10 / DFN@100 time ratio, and the check that a separate DFN@10 run reproduces the first 10 cycles of the DFN@100 run.
- Caveats fixed now: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; K = 10 and m = 3 were picked on development data (K = 5 and 20 were looked at: 5 is as cheap but weaker on Ai2020, 20 costs twice as much for 2 more exact conditions), so the fresh test is the check on that choice.
- Script: `experiments/fade/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5k outcome: the energy optimum sits where the cell's first limit binds. Above the salt flag thermodynamic energy orders first-cycle energy (Spearman 0.99 to 1.00), below it r_kt orders utilisation (0.60 to 0.88), and the two pull against each other; on Ai2020 fade is the first limit and the best moves off the edge (r_kt 0.67)
- Bench: 389 runs (64 Sobol seed-9 designs x SPMe V0 and DFN@100 x Ecker2015@1.5x, Mohtat2020@3x, Ai2020@0.375x, plus 5 V1 re-solves), 3 workers, none killed. All three passed the probe. Two Ai2020 DFN runs did not reach 100 cycles (designs 9 and 47, dropped). U and r_kt for a pool took 20 to 23 s (0.3 s per design, mostly PyBaMM's parameter evaluation).
- H74 supported: among sound designs (r_kt >= 0.30; 13, 36 and 42 of them) Spearman(U, BOL Wh/kg) is 0.99, 1.00 and 1.00. Median utilisation 0.85 to 0.96.
- H75 supported, one at the bar: among starved designs (r_kt <= 0.10; 27, 13 and 10) Spearman(r_kt, utilisation) is 0.82, 0.60 (Mohtat2020@3x) and 0.88.
- H76 supported: Spearman(U, r_kt) over the pool -0.82, -0.81 and -0.36 (Ai2020 again the weakest, as in development).
- H77 supported: DFN on the first (U, r_kt) Pareto layer, with no SPMe run, comes within 0.9%, 4.7% and 2.7% of the pool's DFN best at 18, 14 and 11 DFN calls. It is exact on none of the three (development: 9 of 21).
- Where the best sits: Ecker2015@1.5x r_kt +0.22, U percentile 0.53, retention 0.80; Mohtat2020@3x r_kt +0.12, U percentile 0.59; Ai2020@0.375x r_kt +0.67, U percentile 0.27. The Ai2020 best lies outside 5j's H72 range ([-0.15, 0.65]), the first of 24 conditions to do so: among its sound designs Spearman(U, retention) is -0.94 (development -0.94 to -0.38 on Ai2020), so U buys first-cycle energy and loses it to fade, and U ranks the 100-cycle score at -0.03. On the other two cells retention moves with U (+0.79) or barely at all (+0.14), and the best sits just above the flag.
- Share of log-score variance (covariance with log score over its variance; sums to 1): Ecker2015@1.5x U 0.21, utilisation 0.42, retention 0.37; Mohtat2020@3x 0.60, 0.14, 0.26; Ai2020@0.375x -0.20, 1.15, 0.05. No verdicts.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, `posthoc.txt`): on the same pools 5j's pre-screen and 5h's band procedure are both exact, at 36, 18 and 40 DFN calls (pre-screen) and 25, 15 and 40 (band), plus SPMe on most designs. The front costs 11 to 18 DFN calls and no SPMe, and gives up 0.9% to 4.7%. Top-U above the flag (k = 5) misses by 0.9%, 4.7% and 6.0%.
- Reading: the answer to 5j's question. The score is U x utilisation x retention. U and r_kt are both set by the same design moves (thicker, denser electrodes store more and starve sooner), so across a pool they are anti-correlated (-0.36 to -0.87 on 24 conditions). Above the flag utilisation is flat (0.85 to 0.98) and U decides; below it utilisation falls with r_kt; the best is the highest-U design before utilisation falls, which is why it sits at r_kt between about 0 and 0.4. Where fade grows with U (Ai2020) retention is the binding limit instead and the best moves to lower U and higher r_kt. Two of the three factors cost 0.3 s per design with no simulation; the third (retention) is what still needs DFN, and it is what made the front miss by up to 4.7%. For an engineer: r_kt is a design constraint (do not push loading past r_kt about 0), U is the objective up to it, and the DFN runs are best spent on fade.
- Caveats: three conditions, 62 to 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); U is the parameter set's thermodynamic energy, so a cell with large ohmic or solid-diffusion losses above the flag (NCA_Kim2011@0.5x in development, utilisation 0.80, Spearman 0.52) would break H74; retention is measured, not predicted; H77's 6% bar came from development.
- Output: `experiments/edge/choose.json`, `probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `posthoc.txt`, `edge.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5k: why does the DFN@100 energy optimum sit at the salt edge? The score split into three factors, two of them from the parameter set alone (plan, written before the fresh bench; H74 to H77 are this entry's)
- Question: 5j found the pool's DFN best has r_kt between -0.06 and +0.58 on all 21 conditions, inside a pool spanning -3.98 to +0.92, and its next step asked why. The score every milestone ranks by (DFN Wh/kg at cycle 100) factors exactly as U x utilisation x retention: U the design's thermodynamic energy over its stack mass (PyBaMM's `calculate_theoretical_energy`, SOC 1 to 0, as in 5j's bound), utilisation the first cycle's delivered Wh/kg over U, retention cycle 100 over cycle 1. U and r_kt need no simulation. The claim to test: above the salt flag U orders the designs' first-cycle energy; below it r_kt orders their utilisation; U and r_kt pull against each other across a pool (thicker, denser electrodes store more and starve sooner), so the best sits where the first constraint binds. Retention is the third factor and has no closed form here.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/edge/dev.txt`, all 21 conditions of 5b to 5j; the factor split had not been computed before): among sound designs (r_kt >= 0.30, conditions with at least 10) Spearman(U, BOL) median 0.99, range 0.52 (NCA_Kim2011@0.5x, utilisation 0.80) to 1.00; among starved designs (r_kt <= 0.10) Spearman(r_kt, utilisation) median 0.90, range 0.64 to 0.95; Spearman(U, r_kt) over each pool -0.87 to -0.36 (Ai2020 -0.36 to -0.48, every other cell below -0.76). Retention is where the cells differ: Spearman(U, retention) among sound designs runs from -0.94 (Ai2020@0.25x) to +0.90 (OKane2022@1x). On Ai2020 a high-U design loses capacity faster, so its DFN best sits at U percentile 0.14 to 0.31 and well above the flag (r_kt 0.23 to 0.47); on the other cells it sits at U percentile 0.70 to 0.95 and r_kt -0.06 to +0.37. A zero-SPMe shortlist (DFN on the first Pareto layer of (U, r_kt), both maximised) finds the pool best exactly on 9 of 21 conditions, median gap 0.6%, worst 5.9%, at 14.8 DFN calls on average; two layers, 13 of 21 at 28.4 calls (5j's pre-screen: exact on all, 28 to 42 DFN calls plus SPMe).
- Fresh conditions, chosen by r_kt alone before any run (`experiments/edge/choose.json`; no labels read): candidates in the listed order (5j's list after the two it used, then two Ai2020 rates no milestone ran), the first three of different cells with at least 10 designs predicted in each regime: Ecker2015@1.5x (27 starved, 13 sound), Mohtat2020@3x (13, 36), Ai2020@0.375x (10, 44). Designs: 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 9 (not used before); SPMe@100 V0 and DFN@100 on each, after a baseline probe, as in 5j. Simulation, mapping and pools imported unchanged.
- H74 (U orders the sound designs' first cycle): Spearman(U, BOL Wh/kg) among designs with r_kt >= 0.30 is at least 0.85 on every fresh condition with at least 10 of them. Refuted if below 0.5 on any.
- H75 (r_kt orders the starved designs' utilisation): Spearman(r_kt, utilisation) among designs with r_kt <= 0.10 is at least 0.6 on every fresh condition with at least 10 of them. Refuted if below 0.3 on any.
- H76 (the two pull against each other): Spearman(U, r_kt) over the pool is at most -0.3 on every fresh condition. Refuted if above 0 on any.
- H77 (a shortlist with no SPMe run): DFN on the first (U, r_kt) Pareto layer finds a design within 6% of the pool's DFN best on every fresh condition. Refuted if more than 10% below on any.
- Also reported, no verdicts: Spearman(U, retention) among sound designs, the share of log-score variance each factor carries, where the best sits (r_kt, U percentile, utilisation, retention), the two-layer front and top-U-above-the-flag shortlists, the cost of U and r_kt per pool.
- Caveats fixed now: three conditions, 64 designs each; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); the regime thresholds are 5i's flag and 6a's 0.30, not tuned; U and r_kt ignore degradation, so retention is measured, not predicted; the shortlist's bar comes from development.
- Script: `experiments/edge/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5j outcome: the salt pre-screen does not make the search cheaper (0.90x and 1.12x the band procedure's time), but it makes it exact: on fresh Chen2020@1.5x the band procedure missed the DFN best by 4.5% and the pre-screen did not; the best design sat on the salt flag on 2 of 2 fresh conditions and 6 of 19 committed ones
- Bench: 256 runs (64 Sobol seed-8 designs x SPMe V0 and DFN@100 x OKane2022@1.25x and Chen2020@1.5x), 3 workers, none killed, none failed DFN, no SPMe early stops (so no V1 re-solves). Both conditions passed the probe. r_kt for each pool took 3.8 s and 2.6 s.
- H70 supported: the pre-screen's answer is the pool's DFN best on both (gap 0.0000).
- H71 mixed: pre-screen wall time over the band procedure's 1.12 on OKane2022@1.25x (28 DFN runs against 22; 53 s of SPMe saved, 6 more DFN runs spent) and 0.90 on Chen2020@1.5x (42 against 36; 86 s of SPMe saved). Neither is outside [0.85, 1.15]. With the 19 development conditions (0.91 to 1.08, median 1.01) the pre-screen neither saves nor costs more than about 12%.
- H72 supported: the DFN best's r_kt is +0.03 (OKane2022@1.25x, pool -0.42 to +0.58) and +0.08 (Chen2020@1.5x, pool -0.65 to +0.51). Both are flagged.
- H73 supported: the flag agrees with the 100-cycle SPMe suspect label on 64 of 64 designs on both conditions.
- What the band procedure did on Chen2020@1.5x (not predicted by any hypothesis): it missed the DFN best by 4.54% (121.8 against 127.6 Wh/kg), as did 5g's iterate step; it is the band stop's first miss on 21 conditions. Mechanism: 5e's rule verifies from the lowest SPMe r_min up and stops after three designs in a row that SPMe scored within 25% of DFN; here that run ended at r_min 0.071, inside the flagged block, one design before the best (design 31, r_min 0.078, SPMe 102.8 against DFN 127.6 Wh/kg: 19% low, so not "mis-scored" by milestone 1's label). Every verified design had r_min <= 0.10, so none was "sound" and the band factor q was floored at 1: the stop had nothing to calibrate SPMe's under-scoring against. The pre-screen verifies every flagged design by construction, so it reached design 31; on that condition it was both exact and cheaper (0.90 of the band's time, 0.93 of all-DFN).
- Discarding flagged designs instead (no DFN for them) would miss the best by 1.9% and 6.5% on the fresh conditions, and by up to 17.0% on the committed ones (OKane2022@2x). Across 21 conditions the best design is flagged on 8.
- Post hoc, the thermodynamic bound (PyBaMM's `calculate_theoretical_energy` from SOC 1 to 0 of each design's parameter values, over the stack mass) against DFN@100 on all 1395 pooled designs: never violated, U/DFN from 1.06 to 14.16 (median 2.89). On the committed pools, 0 of 429 flagged designs have a bound below the pool's DFN best, so a branch-and-bound skip of flagged designs would prune none of them even with the best already known. The plan's dev note (five designs, 4.4 to 8.3) stands as a subset of this.
- Reading: the closed-form flag does not buy wall time inside the search, because the designs it flags are the ones the rule sends to DFN anyway and SPMe is the cheap part of the bill. What it buys is that every flagged design gets a DFN score, and that matters because the best design in a pool sits at the salt edge: its r_kt lies between -0.06 and +0.58 on all 21 conditions, and below the flag on 8. A reduced-model screen that treats salt depletion as a reason to discard (or as a reason to stop early) loses the design it is looking for. For the paper this replaces the run-of-3 stop inside the flagged block with a rule that has no tuning: DFN on every r_kt-flagged design, the 5e rule and band stop above the flag.
- Caveats: two fresh conditions, both of them graphite cells already seen at other currents; 64 designs each; the band's miss is one event; timings from one 4-core machine at 3 workers, where SPMe is 0.14 to 0.55 of DFN's time per run (a faster DFN would make the pre-screen relatively dearer); the 25% mis-scored label, the run-of-3 and the 0.10 flag are unchanged from earlier milestones, not tuned here.
- Output: `experiments/prescreen/choose.json`, `probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `bound.jsonl`, `prescreen.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5j: does 5i's closed-form salt flag, used before any simulation, make the design search cheaper? (plan, written before the fresh bench; H70 to H73 are this entry's)
- Question: milestone 5i turned the trust flag into a formula, r_kt, that costs 0.05 s per design and reproduces SPMe's minimum salt ratio. Its next step asked to put it in front of the search and price it. If a design is already known to starve its electrolyte, its SPMe run is wasted: the rule will verify it with DFN anyway.
- The pre-screen procedure, fixed now (`experiments/prescreen/run.py`, `prescreen`): (1) compute r_kt for every design in the pool; designs with r_kt <= 0.10 go to DFN@100 and get no SPMe run; (2) the rest get SPMe (V0, V1 re-solve of early stops) and 5e's rule (queue and run-of-3 stop) over them alone; (3) 5h's band stop over the whole pool from the union of both verified sets, with the pre-flagged designs carrying no screen score. Wall time: r_kt (measured) + SPMe on the rest + every DFN run. Compared with 5h's band procedure (SPMe on every design) and all-DFN.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/prescreen/dev.txt`, the 19 committed conditions; r_kt was written on these designs in 5i, this procedure's cost had not been computed): answer gap 0 on all 19; wall time against the band procedure median 1.01, range 0.91 to 1.08; against all-DFN at most 1.01. The SPMe time saved on flagged designs is spent again: every flagged design is verified, where the band procedure's run-of-3 can stop inside the flagged block, and the rule's run-of-3 then verifies three more designs above the flag. Two variants were tried on the same data and are not tested here: dropping the rule on the unflagged designs (median 0.93 of the band's time, but it misses the DFN best by 4.4% to 7.2% on all four Ai2020 conditions, where SPMe under-scores sound designs and nothing calibrates the band), and a branch-and-bound skip of flagged designs using the cell's thermodynamic energy (PyBaMM's `calculate_theoretical_energy` over the stack mass) as an upper bound on DFN@100 Wh/kg (on the five OKane2022@2x designs checked the bound holds but sits 4.4 to 8.3 times above the DFN score, so it would prune nothing; the full check is reported with the outcome). Also seen: the pool's DFN best is itself flagged (r_kt <= 0.10) on 6 of 19 conditions, and its r_kt lies between -0.06 and 0.58 on all 19, while pools span -3.98 to 0.92.
- Fresh conditions, chosen by r_kt alone before any run (`choose.json`; no labels read): candidates in the plan's order, the first whose predicted flagged share falls in [0.15, 0.45) and the first, of another cell, in [0.45, 0.80): OKane2022@1.25x (0.375) and Chen2020@1.5x (0.609), multiples of 5b's 48.7 A/m2, neither run before. Designs: 64 Sobol designs, scrambled, seed 8 (not used before), SPMe@100 V0 and DFN@100 on each, V1 re-solves of early stops, one fresh process per run, 3 at a time, after a baseline probe as in 5g. Simulation, mapping, labels and procedures imported unchanged.
- H70 (answer): the pre-screen's answer is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on both conditions. Refuted if more than 2% below on either.
- H71 (no saving, as development says): the pre-screen's wall time over the band procedure's lies in [0.90, 1.10] on both. Refuted if below 0.85 or above 1.15 on either; mixed otherwise.
- H72 (the optimum sits at the salt edge): the pool's DFN best has r_kt in [-0.15, 0.65] on both conditions. Refuted if outside [-0.30, 0.80] on either.
- H73 (the flag on 100-cycle labels): r_kt <= 0.10 agrees with the 100-cycle SPMe suspect label (V0 fails or whole-run r_min <= 0.10) on at least 95% of designs on each condition. Refuted below 90% on either.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the gap of a screen that discards flagged designs (best DFN among r_kt > 0.10), calls, the SPMe time saved, the iterate step.
- Caveats fixed now: two conditions, 64 designs each, one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); one 4-core machine's timings; the procedure and H71's range come from the 19 development conditions.
- Script: `experiments/prescreen/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5i outcome: SPMe's salt-depletion flag needs no SPMe run. A closed-form steady state with D_e(c) gives SPMe's minimum salt ratio to within 0.0003 on 408 fresh runs and misclassifies none; the constant-D form ranks as well but over-predicts depletion
- Bench: 816 fresh runs (4 cells x 6 currents x 17 designs x SPMe V0 and DFN, 2 cycles), 3 workers, none killed; 8 Ramadass2004 DFN runs did not finish 2 cycles (dropped from DFN comparisons only). Committed: 1280 designs over the 19 conditions, scored in 60 s of parameter evaluation. Every committed run's current matched the predictor's to 1e-6.
- H64 supported: Spearman(r_ss, SPMe r_min) 0.99 pooled, at least 0.77 in every condition (19 of 19 at least 0.6).
- H65 supported: AUC 0.997; the rule r_ss <= 0.10 misclassifies 13.8% (all on the side of calling a design suspect that is not).
- H66 mixed: the predicted suspect share is within 0.15 on 12 of 19 conditions. It is always too high: at r_ss 0.1 the median SPMe r_min is 0.26, because D_e rises as the salt empties.
- H67 supported: AUC for milestone 1's mis-scored label 0.951 against 0.957 for SPMe's own simulated r_min.
- H68 supported: on the fresh sweep AUC 0.998, misclassification 4.4%, Spearman 1.00; the baseline crossing is predicted at 0.23x (Ramadass2004, every multiple suspect), 0.51x (NCA_Kim2011, observed between 0.5x and 1x), 2.69x (Marquis2019, not crossed by 3x) and 5.0x (Mohtat2020, not crossed by 3x). Three of the four brackets are open-ended, so the crossing part is a weak test, as the plan's factor allowed.
- H69 supported (the post hoc Kirchhoff form r_kt, registered before the fresh labels were read): on the fresh sweep the rule r_kt <= 0.10 misclassifies 0 of 408 designs, and the mean |max(r_kt, 0) - max(SPMe r_min, 0)| is 0.0003 (linear form 0.034). On the committed data, where it was written, it misclassifies 3 of 1280 (all within 0.001 of the threshold), matches every condition's suspect share within 0.02 (19 of 19 within 0.15), has mean difference 0.0031, gives AUC 0.965 for mis-scoring, and with 5g's gate rule (4 of the first 8 designs suspect) makes the same call as the simulated flag on 19 of 19 conditions (post hoc).
- Where it departs: the 19 committed designs where SPMe's r_min is more than 0.05 above r_kt are all Ai2020@0.25x runs that stopped in the first cycle, before reaching steady state. The formula gives what a finished run would reach.
- Against DFN's own minimum: Spearman 0.98 (committed) and 0.91 (fresh), mean absolute difference 0.034 and 0.029. The formula is a model of SPMe's electrolyte, which shares its uniform reaction; DFN's reaction moves away from the depleted collector, so DFN's minimum is higher where SPMe empties.
- Reading: the trust test of milestones 1 to 5h is a function of the parameter set and the design alone: thicknesses, porosities, Bruggeman exponents, D_e(c) and t+. For a pool of designs it costs about 0.05 s per design, against 1 to 30 s for the SPMe run it replaces, and it gives an engineer a closed-form current at which a design empties its electrolyte under SPMe's assumptions. The screen still needs SPMe's score, but the decision of which designs to verify with DFN, and 5g's skip-SPMe gate, can be made before any simulation. It does not say how far SPMe's score is from DFN's; that is still the DFN run's job.
- Process note: an early `report` call, made while the fresh bench had a few dozen rows, wrote a partial `fresh.jsonl` to disk (committed in the second commit); its fresh section was not printed or read before H69 was registered.
- Caveats: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1), whose SPMe uses the same electrolyte equation the formula solves, so the agreement is by construction up to the transient, t+(c) and solver effects; t+ held at c0; four fresh cells, three of them seen before on other designs; isothermal.
- Output: `experiments/sand/committed.jsonl`, `bench.jsonl`, `fresh.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `sand.svg`; derivation in `THEORY.md` part 3.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5i: can a closed-form salt-depletion number, computed from the parameter set and the design alone, say where SPMe breaks before any simulation? (plan, written before the run; H64 to H68 are this entry's)
- Question: since milestone 1 the trust flag is SPMe's own minimum salt ratio r_min, which needs an SPMe run. Milestone 5g found that the gate's cost hinges on how many designs in a pool are salt-starved, and its next steps asked for current density as a continuous axis and a dimensionless number that predicts the crossing without simulation. This step derives one and tests it against every committed label.
- The predictor, fixed now (`experiments/sand/run.py`, `r_ss`): the steady state of the linear electrolyte problem under a uniform reaction in each electrode (the profile SPMe's leading-order correction relaxes to). With q = (1 - t+) i / F, effective diffusivity D_k = D_e(c0, T0) eps_k^b_k in each region, and zero flux at both collectors, the profile is piecewise quadratic-linear-quadratic, shifted so its porosity-weighted mean is c0. r_ss = min over discharge and charge (same current) of min c / c0, which is linear in current: r_ss = 1 - I / I_ss. D_e and t+ are evaluated at c0 and the set's initial temperature; nothing is fitted. A design is predicted suspect when r_ss <= 0.10 (milestone 2's flag threshold, unchanged).
- Secondary predictor, no verdicts: r_t = 1 - (1 - r_ss)(1 - exp(-t_d / tau)), with tau the slowest mode's time constant (porosity-weighted L^2 / (pi^2 D_eff)) and t_d the nominal discharge time scaled by positive loading (Q_nom x eps_s,p L_p / its set's own, over the current).
- Data, committed: the 19 conditions of milestones 5b to 5h (seven cells at 1x, 5g's six, 5h's six), 64 designs each, SPMe@100 (V0) and DFN@100. Labels as committed: suspect = SPMe returned nothing or whole-run r_min <= 0.10; mis-scored = milestone 1's label (SPMe score <= 0 or 25% below DFN@100), on 5e's pool (designs whose DFN finished). The predictor has seen none of these numbers; the shares and counts in this log have been read.
- Data, fresh: three cells no milestone has used, ORegan2022, Prada2013 (LFP) and Ramadass2004 (SPMe fails at the baseline design in 5e's probe), at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2 and 3 times 48.7 A/m2, on 16 Sobol designs (scrambled, seed 7, not used before) plus the baseline design, SPMe (V0) and DFN for 2 cycles each, one fresh process per run. Simulation, mapping and copied degradation keys are 5g's, imported unchanged. Runs that fail or stop early are labels (SPMe) or dropped and counted (DFN), as before.
- Amendment, before any fresh run returned a label: a baseline probe (SPMe and DFN for 2 cycles at 1x) found that ORegan2022 and Prada2013 (and Chen2020_composite and Xu2019, tried as replacements) fail inside 5b's design mapping on missing parameters (for example `Bulk solvent concentration`, which the mapping does not copy), so no run of theirs is a label. Ramadass2004 runs (DFN finishes, SPMe fails structurally, as in 5e's probe). The fresh sweep is therefore Ramadass2004 plus three committed cells on fresh designs and currents: Marquis2019, Mohtat2020 and NCA_Kim2011 (the cells whose committed suspect shares are lowest, so the sweep up to 3x can show a crossing), same rates, seed 7, 17 designs. H68's crossing test runs over these four cells (factor-2 misses on two of four refute it). Fresh here means designs and currents no milestone has run, and for three of the four cells not a fresh cell.
- Second amendment, written after the committed verdicts (H64 to H67) were computed and before any fresh-sweep label was read (the bench was still running): the linear r_ss ranks the committed designs almost perfectly but sits below SPMe's r_min (at r_ss 0.1 the median SPMe r_min is 0.26), and the transient is not the cause (the discharge lasts at least 6 times the slowest diffusion time). D_e(c) rises as the salt empties in most sets, which a constant-D formula misses. The steady state with D_e(c) is still closed form through the Kirchhoff variable Phi(c) = int_0^c D_e: the flux equation is linear in Phi, so Phi has the piecewise profile above with eps^b in place of D_eff, and one bisection imposes mass conservation in c (`r_kt` in `experiments/sand/run.py`; t+ still at c0). On the committed data, seen while writing it, r_kt misclassifies 3 of 1280 designs. H69 (pre-registered for the fresh sweep only): the rule r_kt <= 0.10 misclassifies at most 2% of fresh designs, and the mean absolute difference between max(r_kt, 0) and max(SPMe r_min, 0) on designs where SPMe ran is at most 0.02. Refuted if misclassification is above 5% or the mean difference above 0.05.
- H64 (rank): Spearman(r_ss, SPMe r_min) over committed designs where SPMe returned a run is at least 0.8 pooled, and at least 0.6 within at least 15 of the 19 conditions. Refuted if pooled below 0.6.
- H65 (classify without simulation): pooled AUC of -r_ss for the suspect label is at least 0.90, and the fixed rule r_ss <= 0.10 misclassifies at most 15% of committed designs. Refuted if AUC below 0.80 or misclassification above 25%.
- H66 (the gate's input): per condition, the predicted suspect share is within 0.15 of the observed share on at least 15 of 19 conditions. Refuted on fewer than 10.
- H67 (what matters, mis-scoring): pooled AUC of -r_ss for the mis-scored label is at least 0.80 and within 0.05 of the AUC of SPMe's own r_min (the simulated flag, failures counted as r 0). Refuted if below 0.70.
- H68 (fresh cells, continuous current): on the fresh sweep, pooled AUC of -r_ss for the suspect label (2-cycle SPMe) is at least 0.90, and for each fresh cell the baseline design's predicted crossing multiple (r_ss = 0.10) lies within a factor 1.5 of the bracket the sweep observes (last multiple not suspect, first suspect). Refuted if the AUC is below 0.80 or the crossing misses by more than a factor 2 on two of the three cells.
- Also reported, no verdicts: Spearman with DFN's r_min, r_t's AUCs, per-cell I_ss for the baseline design, and the misclassified designs.
- Caveats fixed now: the predictor ignores concentration dependence beyond c0, the non-uniform reaction (DFN's), the transient (except r_t) and the solid phase; the committed shares were read before the predictor was written, so H64 to H67 test a parameter-free formula on seen data, and only H68 is on unseen data; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1).
- Script: `experiments/sand/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6a outcome: against measured discharges, swapping SPMe for DFN never moves capacity by more than 1.5 points while the parameter set is off by up to 4.3; SPMe's r_min does not order the gap across cells; the cell heats by 10 K at 2C, and once that is modelled DFN's apparent 2C win over SPMe shrinks from 1.5 to 0.3 points
- Bench: 18 runs (SPM, SPMe, DFN x 6 conditions), none failed, 0.1 to 0.4 s each; measured files from pybamm-data v1.0.2, hash-checked by `pybamm.DataLoader`. Measured capacity to V_cut: Ecker2015 0.1610 Ah (1C), 0.1521 (5C); Ai2020 2.3323, 2.3077, 2.2788, 2.2258 Ah (0.1C to 2C).
- H61 supported: SPMe is never closer to the measured capacity than DFN by more than 0.13 points (Ecker2015@5C, SPMe -2.62% against DFN -2.75%).
- H62 mixed: Spearman(SPMe r_min, excess error) = -0.14 over the six. The two lowest r_min values tell opposite stories: Ecker2015@5C at r_min 0.42 has SPMe and DFN within 0.13 points, Ai2020@2C at 0.44 has them 1.50 points apart. As in milestone 5b, r_min ranks within a cell but its level does not carry across cells.
- H63 supported: on all four conditions with SPMe r_min >= 0.5, |Q_SPMe - Q_DFN| (at most 0.06% of measured) is smaller than DFN's own error (0.88% to 4.26%). Where the flag trusts SPMe, the parameter set, not the model reduction, is what separates the model from the cell.
- Prior work found after the run: `research/energy-cell-dfn` (2026-11-11) had run the same six comparisons on the same files, by voltage RMSE and with a lumped thermal model. This run's numbers reproduce its default-mesh ones to 0.1 mV (for example Ai2020@2C DFN 18.6 against 17.9 on its 4x mesh; lumped 46.3 against 46.3), from separate code. What is new here is the capacity framing with pre-registered tests tied to the trust flag (H61 to H63), and the post hoc items below; that project had left open whether the Ai cell was held at temperature.
- Post hoc (after the verdicts, not pre-registered): (1) Ai2020 is +4.2% to +5.0% on capacity under every model at every rate, 0.1C included, so the set carries a rate-independent offset against this cell. Dividing it out (capacity at each rate over capacity at 0.1C), the measured 2C retention is 0.9543; isothermal SPM is +0.65 points, SPMe -0.60, DFN -1.98. (2) PyBaMM's lumped thermal model gives a peak temperature rise of 1.64, 4.06 and 10.37 K at 0.5C, 1C and 2C; the measured files (`*_discharge_T.txt`) give 1.57, 4.04 and 10.41 K. The cell was not isothermal. Whether the Ai2020 thermal parameters were fitted to these temperatures was not checked; if they were, the match is expected rather than independent. With lumped thermal the 2C retention errors are SPM +0.75, SPMe -0.17, DFN -0.47 points, the SPMe-DFN capacity gap falls from 1.50 to 0.32 points, and DFN's 2C voltage RMSE rises from 18.6 to 46.3 mV (SPMe 66.6). So DFN's isothermal 2C fit, the best number in either project, is a heating error cancelling the +4% offset. On Ecker2015@5C lumped thermal improves every model (DFN -2.75% to -1.78%, 85.6 to 57.9 mV).
- Reading: on two commercial cells at up to 5C, the choice between SPMe and DFN moves delivered capacity by at most 1.5 points (0.3 once heating is modelled), while the published parameter set misses by up to 4.3 points and the thermal option moves the answer by up to 1.7. For the programme this bounds what DFN verification buys on mildly depleted cells (r_min 0.3 to 1): it is real but second order to parametrisation and temperature. The salt-depleted designs where SPMe fails outright (r_min near 0, milestones 1 to 5) are not reached by any measured curve here, so H4 (the design ranking against measured cells) is still open.
- Caveats: default mesh only (`energy-cell-dfn` found Ecker2015@5C moves 15 mV between 1x and 4x meshes); two cells, discharge only; the parameter sets were built on these cells; the measured temperature is likely a surface reading, against the model's volume average.
- Output: `experiments/measured/bench.jsonl`, `bench_lumped.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `measured.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6a: against measured discharges of two commercial cells, is DFN closer than SPMe, and does SPMe's own salt flag say where it departs from the measurement? (plan, written before the run; H61 to H63 are this entry's)
- Question: every milestone so far scored SPMe against DFN. Milestone 6 (H4) asks whether the fidelity ranking holds against measured cells. No measured design sweep is reachable from this session, so this first step checks the three model forms against the measured constant-current discharges PyBaMM's data registry ships (pybamm-data v1.0.2, fetched through `pybamm.DataLoader` and checked against its SHA-256 sums): the Kokam SLPB 75106100 cell of Ecker et al. (2015) at 1C and 5C (digitised curves, about 30 points each), and the Enertech pouch cell of Ai et al. (2020) at 0.1C, 0.5C, 1C and 2C (1 s sampling).
- Models and settings, fixed now: PyBaMM 26.10.1.1 SPM, SPMe and DFN with default options (isothermal, no degradation), the shipped `Ecker2015` and `Ai2020` parameter sets with their own initial concentrations, current = C-rate x the set's nominal capacity (0.15625 Ah and 2.28 Ah), model cut-off 2.5 V (Ecker2015) and 3.0 V (Ai2020). r_min is each run's minimum electrolyte concentration over time and space over the initial 1000 mol/m3.
- Metrics: delivered capacity to a common cut-off V_cut (2.8 V Ecker2015, 3.05 V Ai2020, above every measured end voltage), as the relative error (Q_model - Q_meas)/Q_meas; voltage RMSE over the time both curves cover.
- H61 (fidelity order holds against measurement): on every condition DFN's absolute capacity error is at most SPMe's plus 1 percentage point. Refuted if SPMe is closer by more than 2 points on any.
- H62 (the flag points at the departure): SPMe's excess error, |err_SPMe| - |err_DFN|, is ordered by SPMe's r_min: Spearman(r_min, excess) <= -0.6 over the six conditions. Refuted if above 0. (Six points; a weak test, stated as such.)
- H63 (where SPMe is trusted, parametrisation dominates): on conditions with SPMe r_min >= 0.5, |Q_SPMe - Q_DFN| is smaller than |Q_DFN - Q_meas| on at least two thirds of them. Refuted if on fewer than one third.
- Also reported, no verdicts: SPM's errors, DFN r_min, voltage RMSE per model.
- Caveats fixed now: both parameter sets were fitted or measured on these same cells (Ai2020's on these very discharges, as far as its paper reports), so this is a check of model form under the authors' own parametrisation, not an out-of-sample test; the models are isothermal and the cells are not; two cells, six conditions; one PyBaMM version.
- Script: `experiments/measured/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5h outcome: the band stop returns the DFN best on all six new conditions at no more than 1.07x all-DFN, but the iterate step it was built to fix also does, so the fix is insurance not shown to be needed on fresh data
- Bench: 788 runs (64 Sobol seed-6 designs x SPMe V0 and DFN@100 x 6 conditions, plus 20 V1 re-solves), 3 workers, no memory kills. All six conditions passed the probe. On Ai2020@0.25x, 13 of 64 DFN runs failed in the first cycle (IDA_ERR_FAIL, minimum step size) and were dropped as the plan says, leaving 51 designs; every other DFN run reached 100 cycles.
- H56 supported: the band answer is the pool's DFN best on all six (gap 0.0000).
- H57 supported: band wall time over all-DFN 1.07 (Ai2020@0.75x), 0.72, 0.61, 0.57, 0.33, 0.70. The one above 1 is a cell where SPMe mis-scores 48 of 64 designs.
- H58 refuted: two conditions had a pool median DFN/SPMe ratio over sound designs of at least 1.10 (Ai2020@0.75x 1.315; the others 1.00 to 1.05), and the iterate step reached the DFN best on both, as it did on all six. So the failure the band stop was built for (5g's Ai2020@0.5x, 3.2% short) did not recur on fresh designs, and the stop's need is not shown.
- What the insurance cost: 9 more DFN runs than the iterate step on Ai2020@0.75x (60 against 51, wall time 1.07 against 0.93 of all-DFN), 11 more on Ai2020@0.25x (31 against 20; 0.72 against 0.51), 1 more on Ecker2015@0.5x, none on the other three.
- H59 supported: rule recall 1.00 on Ai2020@0.75x (48 mis-scored), Ai2020@0.25x (17) and NCA_Kim2011@0.5x (16), 0.97 on OKane2022@1.5x (29 of 30; the missed design 57 has r_min 0.065, SPMe 28% below DFN, and DFN 3.7% below the pool best, so the answer was unaffected). Chen2020@0.5x and Ecker2015@0.5x had no mis-scored design.
- H60 supported: Ai2020's suspect share is 0.06 at 0.25x (at most 0.31) and 0.53 at 0.75x (between 0.31 and 0.72). With 5g and 5f the share is 0.06, 0.31, 0.53, 0.72 at 0.25x, 0.5x, 0.75x, 1x: monotone in current on four different design draws.
- Reading, across all 19 conditions now run (13 development, 6 fresh): the iterate step misses the DFN best on 1 (Ai2020@0.5x, 3.2%); the band stop misses on none, at a cost of 0 to 13 extra DFN runs per pool (up to 0.21 of all-DFN wall time). Whether that is worth it depends on how much a 3% miss costs a designer against a fifth more simulation time; neither answer step is shown to dominate. Current again moved SPMe's reliability more than the change of cell: at half current Chen2020 and Ecker2015 had no mis-scored design at all.
- Output: `experiments/band/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `band.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5h: does a gap-aware stop fix the answer step where SPMe under-scores sound designs? (plan, written before the run; H56 to H60 are this entry's)
- Question: milestone 5g left one open problem. On Ai2020@0.5x SPMe under-scored almost every sound design (median 24% below DFN), so the iterate answer step compared a verified DFN score with biased-low SPMe scores and stopped 3.2% short of the pool's DFN best. 5g's own next step: keep verifying while an unverified design's SPMe score is within the observed SPMe-DFN gap of the verified best.
- The band stop, fixed now: start from milestone 5e's verified set (rule unchanged). Let b be the verified design with the best DFN@100 and q the median DFN/SPMe ratio over verified *sound* designs (positive screen score and r_min > 0.1), floored at 1 (1 if none). While some unverified design with a positive screen score has score x q >= DFN(b), verify the one with the highest screen score, then recompute b and q. The answer is b, so it is always a verified design. Wall time: every SPMe run (V0 plus V1) plus every DFN run the rule and the stop make.
- Seen before this entry (development, `experiments/band/dev.txt`): the stop was chosen on the 13 committed conditions (5g's six and the seven 1x cells) from four variants (median or max ratio; sound or all verified designs). The median-over-sound form returns the DFN best on all 13 (gap 0) and costs the same calls as the iterate step on 11; on Ai2020@0.5x it takes 47 calls against 34 (wall time 0.87 of all-DFN) and on Ai2020@1x 62 against 61. The max-ratio forms also reach the best but verify the whole pool on up to 7 conditions. So nothing below is new evidence about those 13.
- New conditions, in this order, none used by any milestone: Ai2020 0.75x, Ai2020 0.25x, NCA_Kim2011 0.5x, Chen2020 0.5x, Ecker2015 0.5x, OKane2022 1.5x (multiples of milestone 5b's 48.7 A/m2). Low currents were chosen because SPMe's under-scoring appeared at Ai2020's lower current. A probe (baseline design, SPMe at V0 and DFN for 2 cycles) keeps each condition where both finish.
- Designs: 64 Sobol designs (scrambled, seed 6, not used before) per kept condition; SPMe@100 at V0 and DFN@100 on every design, one fresh process per run, 3 at a time; V1 re-solves of every early stop; designs whose DFN does not reach 100 cycles are dropped and counted. Simulation, mapping, protocol and labels are 5g's (`experiments/gate/run.py`), imported unchanged.
- H56 (answer): the band answer's DFN@100 is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every kept condition. Refuted if more than 2% below on any.
- H57 (cost): the band procedure's wall time is at most 1.15 x all-DFN on every condition. Refuted if above 1.30 on any.
- H58 (the problem recurs): on at least one condition where the pool's median DFN/SPMe ratio over sound designs is at least 1.10, 5g's iterate answer misses the DFN best by more than 1%. Refuted if it misses by at most 1% on every such condition; untestable if none has a ratio of 1.10. (If refuted, the band stop's need is not shown on fresh data, whatever H56 says.)
- H59 (recall): on each condition with a mis-scored design, the 5e rule verifies at least 90% of them. Refuted if below 80% on any.
- H60 (current orders Ai2020's suspect share): at 0.25x the share is at most 0.31 (5g's 0.5x) and at 0.75x it lies between 0.31 and 0.72 (the 1x share), each with 0.05 slack. Refuted if either misses by more than 0.10; between is mixed.
- Also reported, no verdicts: calls and wall time of both answer steps, q at the stop, mis-scored counts, the pool's median ratio.
- Caveats fixed now: the stop was read off the 13 development conditions; one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 64 designs per condition; current is the only condition changed.
- Script: `experiments/band/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5g outcome: current, not the cell, decides how often SPMe breaks (Chen2020 25% to 81% at 2x); the registered gate picks wrong on 2 of 6 conditions but never costs more than 1.13x; the iterate answer misses by 3.2% where SPMe under-scores everything
- Parallel work and numbering: this step was planned and run alongside milestone 5f (#15350, merged while this bench ran), which neither run saw. Both registered H46 to H50; this step's are renumbered H51 to H55 here with no other change to the plan's text, and the step is renamed 5g. Both used Sobol seed 5, so Ecker2015, Mohtat2020 and Ai2020 here run the same 64 designs as 5f's 1x benches: a paired comparison of current on identical designs, which neither plan foresaw.
- Bench: 768 runs (64 Sobol seed-5 designs x SPMe V0 and DFN@100 x 6 conditions) plus 4 V1 re-solves. All six conditions passed the probe; every DFN run reached 100 cycles, so none was dropped. Ten Ai2020@0.5x DFN runs were killed for memory at 4 workers and re-run at 2 (not recorded as failures; their wall times, 28 to 34 s, were measured at 2-way concurrency, the other 54 at 4). Mohtat2020@2x had 4 early SPMe stops; V1 finished all 4 and none was mis-scored.
- H55 supported: the suspect share moves with current on every cell. At 2x: OKane2022 0.18 to 0.81, Chen2020 0.25 to 0.81, Ecker2015 0.06 to 0.70, Marquis2019 0.00 to 0.22, Mohtat2020 0.00 to 0.02; Ai2020 at 0.5x falls from 0.72 to 0.31. At 2x OKane2022 and Chen2020 fail on the same 43 designs (OKane2022 is Chen2020's cell with SiOx and fitted degradation, which do not enter the first discharge's salt balance).
- H51 refuted: the gate skipped SPMe on OKane2022@2x (7 of 8 suspect), Chen2020@2x (7) and Ecker2015@2x (5), and was wrong on OKane2022 (rule/all-DFN 0.94) and Ecker2015 (0.90); right on Chen2020 (1.07), Marquis2019@2x (0.78), Mohtat2020@2x (0.43) and Ai2020@0.5x (0.66). None was within 5%, so all six count. The cause: a structural SPMe failure costs under a second (median V0 time 0.9 s on OKane2022@2x against 27.4 s for DFN), so a pool full of failures makes SPMe cheap, not expensive. The suspect share alone is the wrong signal; the time ratio matters as much.
- H52 supported: the gated wall time is 1.00 to 1.13 times the cheaper branch on every condition (worst Ecker2015@2x). Being wrong costs little because the two branches are close exactly where the gate is unsure.
- H53 supported: the rule verified every mis-scored design on both conditions where the gate ran it and some design was mis-scored (Marquis2019@2x 10 of 10, Ai2020@0.5x 31 of 31). Recall was also 1.00 on the three skipped conditions, had the rule run.
- H54 refuted: the gated answer is the DFN best on five conditions, but 3.2% short on Ai2020@0.5x (117.4 against 121.2 Wh/kg). There SPMe under-scores almost every design (median 24% below DFN, no failures), the DFN-best design sits 17% low at r_min 0.47, 42nd in the verify queue, and the rule stops at r_min 0.39 after three sound designs. The iterate step then compares a verified DFN score with SPMe scores that are now biased low, so the unverified best never leads. 5e's pre-registered answer misses there too (3.2%), and on Ecker2015@2x by 10.3%, Mohtat2020@2x 2.8% and Marquis2019@2x 0.4%, where the iterate answer reaches the best.
- Paired with 5f's 1x runs on the same designs (after the verdicts): every design whose suspect status changed moved the way H55 predicted. Ecker2015: 39 designs became suspect at 2x and none recovered (share 0.09 to 0.70); Ai2020: 25 recovered at 0.5x and none broke (0.70 to 0.31); Mohtat2020: 1 became suspect (0.00 to 0.02). On Ai2020 5f's verified answer step returned the DFN best in all 30 draws at 1x; on the same designs at 0.5x it is 3.2% short, so 5f's reading that the answer step is settled holds at 1x only.
- Extra, after the verdicts (post hoc, 13 conditions including the seven 1x cells): (1) a cost-aware gate that also runs DFN on design 0 and predicts rule/all-DFN as mean SPMe time of the first 8 over that DFN time plus the suspect share is right on all six new conditions and on 6 of 7 at 1x (wrong on Ai2020@1x, predicting 0.90 against 1.10). (2) Comparing like with like, verifying every unverified design whose SPMe score is at least the best verified design's, reaches the best on 12 of 13 (Ecker2015@2x 0.9%) but verifies all 64 designs on four conditions, so it is no cheaper than all-DFN where failures abound. (3) An affine map from SPMe to DFN fitted on the verified designs is worse (gaps up to 14.6% on Marquis2019@2x), because the verified designs are the salt-depleted ones, and the line does not extrapolate to the sound ones.
- Relation to 5f's switch: 5f's pilot switch is cost-aware (its prediction adds the SPMe/DFN time ratio from 2 pilot DFN runs), close to this step's post hoc cost-aware gate; this step's registered gate used the suspect count alone, and its refutation is the argument for 5f's form. 5f's conclusion, run the rule every time, agrees with H52 here (at most 1.13x) at currents 5f did not test.
- Reading: whether SPMe can be trusted is a property of the operating point as much as of the cell. Doubling the current turns a cell where SPMe is mostly right into one where it fails on four designs in five. The screen half of the rule kept full recall at every condition. The decision to skip SPMe is cheap to get wrong (at most 13% here), so a designer can run the rule by default. The open problem is the answer: an SPMe bias in either direction breaks a mixed-fidelity comparison, and the fixes tried so far are either expensive or worse. The next step should pre-register a stopping rule that keeps verifying while unverified designs' SPMe scores are within the observed SPMe-DFN gap of the verified best.
- Output: `experiments/gate/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `gate.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5g: can the SPMe run itself say when to skip SPMe, and does the verified-answer rule hold at C-rates no number has been seen for? (plan, written before the run; H51 to H55 are this entry's)
- Question: milestone 5e left two things. On the salt-limited cells (Ai2020, NCA_Kim2011) the two-part rule costs more wall time than running DFN on every design, so a designer needs to know from the cheap runs, early, when to skip SPMe; and the pre-registered answer step mixed fidelities, so the corrected one (verify the merged leader until it is a verified design) needs a test on data it was not read off. Salt depletion is driven by current, so this step moves to operating conditions no run has used: the same cells at a different current.
- Seen before this entry (post hoc, on the seven committed 1x cells): the share of *suspect* designs (SPMe at default tolerances returns nothing, or its whole-run r_min <= 0.1) is 0.00 to 0.25 on the five cells where the rule is cheaper and 0.72 and 0.94 on Ai2020 and NCA_Kim2011; among the first 8 designs in Sobol order it is at most 1 of 8 on the first group and 6 and 8 of 8 on the second. The gate below was read off these numbers.
- The gated procedure, fixed now: (G) run SPMe@100 at V0 on designs 0 to 7 in Sobol index order; if at least 4 of the 8 are suspect, stop using SPMe and run DFN@100 on the whole pool (answer: the DFN best; wall time: those 8 SPMe runs plus every DFN run). (R) Otherwise run milestone 5e's rule unchanged (V1 re-solve of early stops, failures first, then ascending r_min, stop after 3 consecutive sound designs) and then the *iterate* answer step: while the leader of the merged scores (DFN where verified, screen score elsewhere) is unverified, verify it; the answer is that leader. Wall time: every SPMe run (V0 plus V1) plus every DFN run the rule and the answer step make. Early stops are not suspect (milestone 5d: they are the solver's).
- Conditions, in this order: OKane2022 at 2x the areal current density, Chen2020 2x, Marquis2019 2x, Ecker2015 2x, Mohtat2020 2x, Ai2020 0.5x (milestone 5b's 48.7 A/m2 is 1x; 2x is about a 2C rate on the OKane2022 cell). A probe (`experiments/gate/run.py probe`: the baseline design, SPMe at V0 and DFN for 2 cycles) keeps every condition where both finish both cycles. Mapping, protocol, voltage window, degradation options and copied keys are milestone 5b's; only the current is multiplied.
- Designs: 64 Sobol designs (scrambled, seed 5, not used before) per kept condition; SPMe@100 at V0 and DFN@100 on every design, one fresh process per run, 4 at a time; V1 re-solves of every early stop. Designs whose DFN run does not reach 100 cycles are dropped and counted (5e's rule). Wall times are each run's own, measured under the same 4-way concurrency for both models.
- Labels: *mis-scored* is milestone 1's (screen score 0 or more than 25% below DFN@100), on the score after the V1 re-solve.
- H51 (gate decision): on each condition the gate picks the branch that is cheaper in measured wall time (rule with the iterate answer, against all-DFN); conditions where the two are within 5% do not count. Refuted if it picks wrong on 2 or more counted conditions; 1 is mixed; untestable if none counts.
- H52 (gate cost): the gated wall time is at most 1.15 times the cheaper branch's on every condition. Refuted if above 1.30 on any.
- H53 (recall at new currents): on each condition where the gate runs the rule and some design is mis-scored, the rule verifies at least 90% of the mis-scored designs. Refuted if below 80% on any.
- H54 (answer): the gated answer's DFN@100 is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on every condition. Refuted if more than 2% below on any.
- H55 (current drives the suspect share): at 2x each cell's suspect share over its 64 designs is no lower than its committed 1x share minus 0.05 (1x: OKane2022 0.18, Chen2020 0.25, Marquis2019 0.00, Ecker2015 0.06, Mohtat2020 0.00); for Ai2020 at 0.5x it is no higher than 0.72 plus 0.05. Refuted if any condition misses by more than 0.10; between is mixed. (The pools differ, hence the margin.)
- Also reported, no verdicts: each condition's mis-scored count, calls, rule/all-DFN wall-time ratio, the 5e pre-registered answer's gap beside the iterate one, and the gate applied post hoc to the seven 1x cells it was read off.
- Caveats fixed now: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 64 designs per condition; the gate's 8 and 4 and the 0.1 flag are read off seven cells, not tuned on these; current is the only condition changed.
- Script: `experiments/gate/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5f outcome: reading the answer from verified designs returns the DFN best in all 120 draws, and the re-solve holds on fresh stops; the pilot switch misses on Ai2020, but the rule never costs more than 12% over DFN on everything
- Bench: 515 runs (64 Sobol seed-5 designs x SPMe and DFN x 4 cells, plus 3 V1 re-solves), none killed, every DFN run reached 100 cycles, so no design was dropped. SPMe stopped early on 3 Mohtat2020 designs (cycles 8, 20, 76) and failed outright on 50 NCA_Kim2011 designs; mis-scored shares 0% (Mohtat2020), 91% (Ai2020), 80% (NCA_Kim2011), 0% (Ecker2015).
- Fixed branches: rule against all-DFN wall time 185 against 442 s (Mohtat2020), 1393 against 1248 s (Ai2020, all-DFN cheaper by 11.6%, the only decisive cell where it wins), 953 against 968 s (NCA_Kim2011, within 10%), 461 against 949 s (Ecker2015).
- H46 refuted: on Ai2020 the switch fires in 6 of 30 draws (20%). On the other two decisive cells it never fires, which is right (100%). The cause is the suspect cut-off: r* = 0.10 flags 70% of Ai2020's pool while the rule verifies 97%, because 13 of its 58 mis-scored designs have r_min between 0.10 and 0.32. The development replay had shown the same underestimate (0.72 against 0.95) and the plan disclosed it.
- H47 mixed: median wall time 1.08x the cheaper branch on Mohtat2020, 1.12x on Ai2020, 1.03x on NCA_Kim2011 (where the switch fires in 97% of draws though the rule is 1.6% cheaper, so the wrong call costs little), 1.04x on Ecker2015. The overhead on benign cells is the 2 pilot DFN runs, which land on designs the rule would not have verified.
- H48 supported: the returned design is the pool's DFN best in all 120 draws (gap 0), and so is the fixed rule branch's on all four cells. Verifying the leader until it is a verified design fixes 5e's Marquis2019 slip, and it cost 0 to 2 calls more than the queue.
- H49 supported: V1 takes all 3 Mohtat2020 stops to 100 cycles, each within 0.2% of DFN@100 (gaps +0.001 to +0.002). Second fresh-design confirmation of 5d's reading after 5d's 6 of 6.
- H50 supported: every draw that kept the rule verified every mis-scored design (recall 1.00 on Ai2020 and NCA_Kim2011; none on the other two).
- Also reported: variant A (DFN on all 8 pilot designs) switches right in 80% of Ai2020 draws (median 1.02x) but costs 1.21x and 1.29x on the benign cells, as in the development replay.
- Reading: the answer step and the re-solve are now settled on fresh data. The switch is not worth having as built: on these four cells, and on the seven of the development replay, the gap between the rule and all-DFN is never more than 12% when all-DFN wins, while the rule saves 49 to 64% of the all-DFN time on the six pools with few mis-scored designs (13% on Chen2020). A pilot that spends DFN runs costs 2 to 8% on those pools (this switch) or 10 to 29% (variant A). So the practical procedure is the rule with the verified answer step, run every time; a cell that is salt-limited across most of its design space loses at most about a tenth. A per-cell r* would fix H46 but needs DFN labels, which is variant A's cost.
- Output: `experiments/switch/dev.txt`, `dev.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `switch.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5f: can a pilot of 8 SPMe runs tell a designer, before the sweep, whether to run the rule or DFN on everything, and does reading the answer from verified designs return the DFN best? (plan, written before the run; H46 to H50 are this entry's)
- Question: 5e left two fixes. Its answer step mixed verified DFN scores with biased SPMe ones (Marquis2019, 1.3% short), and on salt-limited cells (Ai2020, NCA_Kim2011) the rule cost 5 to 10% more wall time than running DFN on every design. A designer needs to choose the branch before paying for it.
- The switch (fixed now): draw 8 designs at random from the pool and run SPMe@100 on them (V0, with 5e's V1 re-solve of stops). Run DFN@100 on the first 2 of those 8 in the rule's queue order (failures, then ascending r_min; they are at the front of the verify queue either way). Predicted share of all-DFN wall time = median SPMe time of the pilot's non-failed runs / mean DFN time of the 2 + the pilot's *suspect* share (failed, or r_min < 0.10) + 3/64. If it is 1 or more, run DFN on the other designs and return the best. Otherwise run SPMe on the rest and the 5e rule, with the 2 pilot DFN runs already verified (they still count toward, or reset, the run of 3 sound designs).
- The answer step (fixed now): while the design with the best score (DFN where verified, screen score elsewhere) is unverified, verify it; return it. This is 5e's post hoc "iterate" variant (gap 0 on all seven cells there).
- How the constants were chosen, disclosed: a development replay (`experiments/switch/run.py dev`, `dev.txt`) on the seven committed cells, which is post hoc. A first variant that ran DFN on all 8 pilot designs paid 11 to 28% overhead on benign cells (its DFN runs land on sound designs), and a first version of the queue that skipped pre-verified designs without counting them lost OKane2022 recall (0.87) and the pick (6.1% short); both were changed before this plan. With the frozen switch, the replay gives a median wall time of 1.00 to 1.10 times the cheaper fixed branch on all seven cells and a pick gap of 0 in every draw. The r* = 0.10 suspect share tracked the rule's verified share on five cells (0.18 against 0.17 on OKane2022, 0.25 against 0.23 on Chen2020) and underestimated it on Ai2020 (0.72 against 0.95).
- Cells and designs: Mohtat2020 (the cell with solver stops, so the re-solve gets a second test), Ai2020 and NCA_Kim2011 (salt-limited, where all-DFN won in 5e), Ecker2015 (benign). 64 scrambled Sobol designs with seed 5 (not used before), SPMe@100 at V0 and DFN@100 on every design, V1 on every stop, 5b's mapping, protocol and degradation keys, one fresh process per run. Each draw is replayed from the bench: 30 pilot draws per cell (`random.Random(s).sample`, s = 0 to 29); wall time is summed per-run seconds, as in 5e.
- Fixed branches: *rule* (5e's queue from scratch plus the new answer step) and *all-DFN*. The *cheaper branch* is the one with less wall time; a cell is *decisive* when the two differ by more than 10%.
- H46 (switch): on each decisive cell the switch picks the cheaper branch in at least 80% of draws. Refuted if below 60% on any; untestable if no cell is decisive.
- H47 (cost): the switched procedure's median wall time is at most 1.10 times the cheaper branch on each cell. Refuted if above 1.20 on any.
- H48 (answer): in every draw on every cell the returned design's DFN@100 is within 1% of the pool's DFN best. Refuted if any draw is more than 2% short.
- H49 (re-solve, 5e's H43 again): every stop V1 finishes scores within 25% of DFN@100. Refuted if more than one does not; untestable without stops.
- H50 (recall): in draws that keep the rule, at least 90% of mis-scored designs are DFN-verified on each cell. Refuted if below 80% in any draw.
- Also reported, no verdicts: the switched share, calls, and the variant that runs DFN on all 8 pilot designs.
- Caveats fixed now: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 64 designs per cell; four cells, three of them seen in 5b and 5c on other designs, so this tests fresh designs more than fresh cells; the 25% label and the run of 3 are inherited.
- Script: `experiments/switch/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5e outcome: on two new cells the rule catches every mis-scored design (9 of 9 on Chen2020) for 6 to 23% of the pool in DFN runs; its answer step was wrong once, because it compared verified DFN scores with unverified SPMe ones
- Probe: Ramadass2004's baseline SPMe run fails (every step infeasible at the first cycle; its DFN salt reaches 0), Prada2013 and ORegan2022 fail on missing keys as in 5c, so the plan's order chose Marquis2019 and Chen2020. Bench: 256 runs (64 Sobol seed-4 designs x SPMe and DFN x 2 cells), none killed, every DFN run reached 100 cycles (median 9.3 and 6.2 s), no SPMe run stopped early, so no V1 re-solve was needed. Chen2020's SPMe failed outright on 3 designs (every step infeasible), Marquis2019's on none.
- H42 supported, on one cell: Chen2020 has 9 mis-scored designs (14%), 3 failures and 6 salt-depleted, and the rule verifies all 9. Marquis2019 has none (SPMe within 0.4% of DFN at the median, 5.6% at worst; r_min 0.30 to 0.83), so it does not count.
- H43 untestable: no early stops on either cell.
- H44 mixed: on Chen2020 the pick is the DFN best (gap 0). On Marquis2019 it is 1.3% below it (196.8 against 199.5 Wh/kg). The cause is the answer step, not the screen: SPMe ranks Marquis2019's DFN-best design first, and the rule verified it (it has the lowest r_min but one), but SPMe scores its ten best designs 1 to 6% above DFN (median gap over all 64 is 0.4%), so an unverified design's SPMe score (201.4) beat the verified DFN-best (199.5) and was confirmed instead.
- H45 supported: calls 4 on Marquis2019 (0 mis-scored + 4) and 15 on Chen2020 (9 + 6), 6% and 23% of the pool; with every SPMe run, 45% and 87% of the all-DFN wall time (on Chen2020 a DFN run costs only 1.6 SPMe runs).
- Chen2020's DFN-best design is salt-depleted (r_min 0.09) and SPMe scores it 23% low, just inside the 25% label, ranking it 16th of 64; the top 5 by SPMe all sit 6% or more below the best. The bottom-up queue reaches it because its r_min is low, which a top-k SPMe shortlist would not.
- Extra, after the verdicts: two answer steps that do not mix fidelities. Returning the best DFN score among the designs already verified (no extra call) gives gap 0 on both new cells and on 4 of the 5 replayed cells (NCA_Kim2011 0.2%). Verifying the merged leader until it is a verified design gives gap 0 on all 7 cells at 0 to 2 more calls (Marquis2019 6, NCA_Kim2011 58 of 64).
- Post hoc replay on the five cells the rule was built on (no verdicts): recall 1.00 on the four with mis-scored designs; DFN calls 17%, 8%, 95%, 5% and 88% of the pool (OKane2022, Ecker2015, Ai2020, Mohtat2020, NCA_Kim2011); pick gap 0 except NCA_Kim2011 (0.2%). On the salt-limited Ai2020 and NCA_Kim2011 the rule costs more wall time than running DFN on everything (110%, 105%), because nearly every design is mis-scored and SPMe was run as well.
- Reading: on fresh designs and a cell it was not built on, the screen part of the rule held: it found every design SPMe got wrong, including the DFN-best, at the cost the plan allowed. The pre-registered answer step was the weak part: it compares a verified DFN score with SPMe scores carrying a few per cent of bias. Read the answer from verified designs only, or keep verifying the leader. When most of the pool is mis-scored (Ai2020, NCA_Kim2011), skip SPMe and run DFN.
- Output: `experiments/rule/probe.json`, `bench.jsonl`, `results.txt`, `summary.json`, `rule.svg`.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 5e: does the two-part rule from 5c and 5d (re-solve early stops tightly; DFN-verify failures and low-r_min designs bottom-up) hold on cells no number has been seen for, and does it return the DFN-best design? (plan, written before the run; H42 to H45 are this entry's)
- Question: milestones 5c and 5d built the rule post hoc on five cells. A designer needs to know whether it holds on a new cell, and whether, used as a screen, it hands back the design full DFN would have picked. This step fixes the rule now and runs it on new parameter sets and fresh designs.
- Cells: probe (`experiments/rule/run.py probe`, baseline design, SPMe and DFN for 2 cycles) the candidates Ramadass2004, Marquis2019, Chen2020, Prada2013, ORegan2022 in that order, and take the first two whose SPMe and DFN runs both succeed (Chen2020 is late in the order because OKane2022 is built on it). Prada2013 and ORegan2022 failed 5c's probe on missing keys and are kept only as fallbacks under the same mapping. Milestone 5b's design mapping, protocol, areal current density and copied degradation keys, unchanged.
- Designs: 64 Sobol designs (scrambled, seed 4, not used before) per chosen cell; SPMe@100 at V0 (IDAKLU defaults) and DFN@100 at V0 on every design, one fresh process per run.
- The rule, applied design by design from the SPMe output only: (1) a run that returns 1 to 99 cycles (*stop*) is re-solved once at V1 (IDAKLU rtol 1e-6, atol 1e-8); if V1 returns 100 cycles its score and r_min replace V0's, otherwise it counts as a failure. (2) A run that returns no cycle, or a stop V1 cannot finish (*failure*), gets screen score 0 and goes to the front of the verify queue. (3) The rest are queued by ascending r_min (ties by index). DFN-verify in queue order and stop after 3 consecutive *sound* designs (SPMe score within 25% of DFN@100, milestone 1's label). (4) The pick is the design with the highest score, using DFN@100 where verified and the screen score elsewhere; if it was not verified, one more DFN call confirms it. Calls = verified designs + that confirmation.
- Labels: a design is *mis-scored* when its screen score (after step 1) is 0 or more than 25% below DFN@100. Designs whose DFN@100 run does not return 100 cycles are dropped and counted.
- H42 (recall): on each chosen cell the rule DFN-verifies at least 90% of mis-scored designs. Refuted if below 80% on either; between is mixed. A cell with no mis-scored design is reported and does not count.
- H43 (re-solve): every stop V1 finishes scores within 25% of DFN@100. Refuted if more than one does not; untestable on a cell with no stop.
- H44 (pick): the pick's DFN@100 is within 1% of the pool's DFN@100 best on each cell. Refuted if more than 2% below on either.
- H45 (cost): calls are at most the number of mis-scored designs plus 8 on each cell. Refuted if more than mis-scored plus 16 on either.
- Also reported, no verdicts: calls and wall time as a share of all-DFN; the same rule replayed post hoc on the five committed cells (OKane2022 `cycles`, Ecker2015, Ai2020, Mohtat2020, NCA_Kim2011, using 5d's V1 runs for stops), labelled post hoc because the rule was built on them.
- Caveats fixed now: one PyBaMM version (26.10.1.1); 64 designs per cell; the stop-after-3 constant and the 25% label are inherited, not tuned here.
- Script: `experiments/rule/run.py`; outcome logged above this entry.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 5d outcome: SPMe's early stops are the solver's (tight tolerances finish all 6, each within 0.3% of DFN); its outright failures are SPMe's (salt concentration below zero in 59 of 61, no setting rescues a usable score)
- Bench: 455 SPMe runs (67 census designs and 24 controls x 5 variants) plus 67 one-cycle diagnostics. V0 reproduces every committed cycle count (91 of 91). None killed. A first report keyed designs by (set, index) and merged OKane2022 index 1 of the `fresh` bench (a failure) with index 1 of the `cycles` bench (a control); the rows now carry their bench and the report keys on it, no run repeated.
- H38 supported: V1 (IDAKLU rtol 1e-6, atol 1e-8) and V3 (Casadi "safe", same tolerances) take all 6 Mohtat2020 stops to 100 cycles; V4 (mesh doubled) takes 4; V2 (more Newton iterations and convergence failures allowed) takes none and stops each at the same cycle as V0. So it is the error-test tolerance, not the nonlinear solve, that ends these runs.
- H39 supported: the 6 rescued designs score within 0.3% of DFN@100 (gaps -0.000 to +0.003); none is mis-scored. So on Mohtat2020 every mis-score came from the solver setting: the other 58 designs were already within 2.3% per cycle in 5c (only 8 of them were re-run here, as controls).
- H40 supported: no variant gives a usable score to any of the 61 outright failures. V3 returns 100 cycles on 4 NCA_Kim2011 designs and V1 on one of those, but three score 0 (no energy after cycle 1) and one 30.6 against DFN's 70.4 Wh/kg; all four stay mis-scored. The diagnostic puts SPMe's first-discharge salt concentration below zero on 59 of 61 (median -0.08 of initial, low -0.15), and on 57 the charge step comes back empty: the state the discharge ends in has left the reduced model's domain, so no feasible step follows and PyBaMM discards the finished cycle.
- H41 supported: all 6 stops keep positive salt in the first discharge (0.65 to 0.89 of initial).
